# Claim index: every number, and where it came from

If a number from this work turns up somewhere else — in a summary, a slide, another agent's answer — this is
where to check it. Each row gives the value, how it was obtained, the experiment or resource it came from, and
the file and field that hold the evidence.

**Kind:** `M` measured on the card · `S` simulated from RTL · `F` fitted to measurements · `P` predicted by a
model before it was measured · `D` derived: arithmetic on measured numbers, no new measurement · `R` read from
source code (firmware or RTL) · `X` external source · `A` assumption. Combined kinds (e.g. `M, F`) mean a measured
input and a fitted slope; `P→M` is a prediction later measured.

Where a later session re-measured a number, the older row says so and points to the newer one; quote the newer.
Since 26 October 2026 (Q126) five sections at the end carry **E60–E64** — the bit-flip ladder, the deterministic
transistor flips, the heat movies, the camera half of the ladder and Horace's power limit — which the index had
missed: before that it stopped at E59, while those five experiments' own numbers were in circulation. Three figures
E61 **refuted** are marked dead where their replacements sit: **44.4 fJ per result bit**, **14.2 fJ per carry** and
the **8.94 pJ zero gate on `fmadd.ps`**. And where the flip model's accuracy is quoted, the held-out figure is
E38's **0.906–1.008 W rms, scored FAIL**, not E9's in-sample 0.32 W.
Five further sections, added the same day, carry the rest of **Q126**: **E65** (every bit position, the
flow-M case and the hart ladder, on aifoundry3) and four pieces of desk work with no card time — what the
RTL bench's toggle count counts, the joined load model and its frozen predictions, one transistor flip
priced from the open ASAP7 kit and simulated in SPICE, and the multiply-add put through an open synthesis
flow. Everything in those four is kind `S`, `F`, `D` or `P`: **nothing in them is a measurement of this
chip**, and where they meet a measured number the row says which is which.
Rows that cite R3 for the governor describe the firmware source at `353f20e`; the cards' own trace strings match an
older build (R3), so what the card runs may differ. Since 28 September the section "The governor on the cards' own
build, and DV2" has the cards' builds read from source, E51's development data and, since 29 September, its frozen
validation's verdicts (**val**), "Heat placement" E52's, and "The effect of overheating" E53's pre-registered verdicts and
the re-read record; rows marked **dev** there are development data, not validated. Since 29 September five more sections
hold NV's probe (E54) and the major pass's four pre-registered experiments (E55–E58): there **val a3** is a frozen
verdict on aifoundry3, **val a2** the same frozen test on aifoundry2, run as a third card on the evening of 29 September
after DV2 (E55–E57), and **dev c1** a development value on aifoundry1's card 1, which chose nothing that was later tested. "Sparse
parity on the ET-SoC-1" (E59, 29 September) is an engineering benchmark on one card, not pre-registered; its CPU
energies are **assumed**. Terms are defined in [README.md](README.md#terms). Since 1 November 2026 the section "Q132", before
"Ridge points", carries E92–E106 and what they overturn: older rows it overturns carry a dated note, and hold their old
text as the record. The section "Q133", after it, carries E107–E118 the same way (E117 was not run, and E77 follows its
verification), with Q133(a) and Q133(b) at the desk. The section "Q134", after that, carries E119–E125, Q134(a) at the
desk and E77's corrected result the same way. The section "Q136", after that, carries E126–E134 and Q136's four desk
lanes the same way, E133's among them, verified after the section was first written (holds with fixes, `f50c77fd`).

Paths are relative to the repository root. `DATA` means `docs/reports/data/2026-09-21-horace-aifoundry2/`,
`DATA2` means `docs/reports/data/2026-09-22-dvfs-aifoundry2/`, `DATA3` means
`docs/reports/data/2026-09-22-horace-aifoundry3/` and `DATAC` means `docs/reports/data/2026-09-22-cards/`.

## The DVFS loop and leakage suppression

`DATA2` means `docs/reports/data/2026-09-22-dvfs-aifoundry2/`; `DATA2/dvfs.json` holds the computed tables.

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Governor thresholds | 65 °C software, 65 W TDP, checked once per management pass, ~133 ms on aifoundry2 with nothing polling (135 ms on aifoundry1's card 1, 224 ms on aifoundry3: E41; the 10 ms is the sleep at the end of each pass) | X | R3 | `thermal_pwr_mgmt.h`, `mgmt_build_config.h` (`DM_TASK_DELAY_MS`) |
| The loop's power input is a **measurement**, not an activity estimate | `pmic_read_average_soc_power()` over I2C | X | R3 | `services/thermal_pwr_mgmt.c`, `update_module_soc_power()` |
| The loop has **no hysteresis** | in the `353f20e` source both guardband macros are defined and never used; the older governor the cards' logs point to does use the power guardband, and the thermal test has no dead band in either | X | R3 | `thermal_pwr_mgmt.c` lines 212–223; no other reference in the tree |

## Version 3: the three-card check (E35–E47, 25–26 Sep; E48 after it)

`V3R` is `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/results/`. Each row gives a value per card, aifoundry2 (a2),
aifoundry3 (a3) and aifoundry1's card 1 (a1c1), with its 99% t interval over independent passes (E38's intervals are
corrected for its 19 sub-tests, E40's are 99.75%), and the item's outcomes: **registered**, over a2 and a3 as PLAN3
pre-registered them, and **all cards**, over the three. A FAIL means the prediction failed and the page takes the
measured value, per card; REPORTED means shown, not tested, on that card. How the check ran, and what each item tests:
[03-experiments.md](03-experiments.md), "Version 3 of the claims check" and E35–E47. Where a row here re-measures an
older row's quantity, quote this one with its per-card values; the older sections that were re-measured point here
(the refresh, the idle law, the catalogue, the reruns and the fit, the hot line, the relay, heat per millimetre, the
memory hierarchy and on-chip communication). Every catalogue
entry per card, and pooled over the cards, is in `V3R/catfull.json`, `.configs["<configuration>"]` (E46).

| Launch-temperature offset of the registered ablation values (post-data note C2, revised 26 Sep) | The registered switching values of V3-ABL-A and V3-ABL-B use a fixed reference launch (80.9 °C on a2 and a1c1, 55.8 °C on a3), but the runs launched at whole-degree readings of 80–81, 57–58 and 80 °C. At the die temperature of each launch they move by: V3-ABL-A (E38) a2 0.62 W high to 0.15 W low, a3 0.91–1.43 W low, a1c1 0.73 W high to 0.05 W low; V3-ABL-B (E39) a2 0.49 high to 0.29 low, a3 0.81–1.34 low, a1c1 0.73 high to 0.05 low. The first figure reads the whole-degree reading as the die temperature; the second puts the die 0.96 °C above it (a launch on a downward step of the reading, as on 21 September); the reading cannot tell them apart. So most of a3's registered deficit is the reference offset, not the card: at the same die temperature a3 switches 0.958–0.969 of a2 over the Horace patterns (registered 0.895; the catalogue's independent 0.972), and a1c1 1.004 (registered 1.009). Flip-model scales: registered 0.986 / 0.882 / 0.995, at launch 0.956–0.994 / 0.926–0.952 / 0.959–0.997. ### Gathers, scatters and packed atomics (E48, 26 Sep, three cards)

`GS` is `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/results/gs.json`; `GSF` is `gs-full.json` beside it. Values are pooled
over the three cards' pass values (`[min–max]` over the nine), at 1,024 minions and 600 MHz; "random" is one random
word on each line of a 4 KB tile, the tiles in a scrambled order (03-experiments.md, E48). Energies are over each
card's own idle. E48 ran after the campaign's blocks on the same three cards under its own rules
(`tools/claims-v3/gs/README.md`, fixed before data), so its tested items carry one all-cards outcome, decided per card.
Since 27 September the energy manual (§3.1, 4.3, 4.4, 6), the memory hierarchy ("Irregular access"), the influence
page (S3, K3, K6) and the hub's chart of events carry these, from `manual.json` `gs` (the same pooling); §3.1's bold
figures pool a2 and a3 only (`GSF.combined_catalogue`, the README's rule for rows beside the catalogue), with a1c1 beside.

## The governor on the cards' own build, and DV2 (E51, 28–29 Sep; development, then the frozen validation on the same card)

`DV2` means `docs/reports/data/2026-09-28-dvfs2-aifoundry2/`; `V3` means `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/`.
`recount_v3.py` is `tools/claims-v3/dv2/recount_v3.py` (run from the repository root). Source rows cite et-platform
`ffca4cbb4` (= `cafe03fc3^`, BL2 0.20.0, the closest public source to release 1.3.1) and `da192816a` (BL2 0.18.0,
release 1.2.0), `device-bootloaders/src/ServiceProcessorBL2/`. **dev** = E51's development data: it chose the
frozen validation's parameters and tests nothing. **val** = the frozen validation (`DV2/plan/PREREG-VAL.md`), a
replication on aifoundry2 from 28 September 20:45:39 PDT to 29 September 16:56:46 PDT, reduced by the frozen
`tools/claims-v3/dv2v/reduce_val.py`: its words PASS, FAIL and INSUFFICIENT are final, and a theory survives when every
item under it passes, falls when any fails, and is otherwise untested. `DV2V` means `DV2/validation/`, `VV` its
`verdicts-dv2val.json` (`.items[<item>]`, `.theories`). The **dev** rows stay as the development night's numbers; each
has a **val** row below with the validation's verdict on the same question: quote those.

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## Heat placement (E52, 27–28 Sep; development, calibration and the frozen validation on aifoundry1's card 1)

`HP` means `docs/reports/data/2026-09-28-heat-placement/`; `dev-r3` is `HP/reductions/dev-r3.json` (`reduce.py --dev`
on `HP/raw/aifoundry3`, reproduced byte for byte), `.dev.items[<item>]`. **dev** = development data on aifoundry3: it
chose the frozen validation's parameters and predictions and tests nothing; **cal** = card 1's calibration on a
workload that is never tested; **val c1** = the frozen validation on aifoundry1's card 1 (`HP/val.json`,
`reduce.py --val` on `HP/raw/aifoundry1-c1`, reproduced byte for byte; its words PASS, FAIL, INSUFFICIENT are final).
t66 is the time from the first launch to the first 10 Hz sample with the host's mean at
66 °C (the governor's trip), from the same falling start edge; L = ln(t66 A / t66 B); intervals are 99% t over blocks.
Only signs transfer between cards: the dev and val c1 sizes are each card's own.

## The effect of overheating (E53, 28 Sep; pre-registered, aifoundry3 and aifoundry1's card 1)

| A swing to 120 °C against one to 65 °C (both from 25 °C), in package fatigue (**derived**) | 5.6–7.6 times the damage per cycle (Coffin-Manson, exponent 2 for solder joints in JESD47I Annex A, 2.35 for the package in RAMP §3.4) | D, X | — | `an/derived.json` `coffin_manson` |
| Outside limits, for comparison | throttle points 95–110 °C (Ryzen 7000 95, i9-13900K 100, RX 5700 hotspot 110, Jetson Orin 99/103, shutdown 104.5/105); THERMTRIP near 125 °C; JESD47 qualification 1,000 h at Tj ≥ 125 °C, 0 of 231 may fail; LPDDR4X standard grade 85 °C case; simulated 7 nm crossover (ZTC) about 0.53 V | X | the page's sources | `OH/sources.json` |

Corrections this work makes (the page carries them): the host has **no per-sensor temperature series** (E51 and E52 recorded
the mean, anonymous peak-holds and the I/O-shire sensor, not 34 sensors at 10 Hz); "card 0 throttled 10 times" has no
source (the 10 in 14-card-behaviour.md, "Selecting one card on a two-card host", counts aborted `dev_mngt_service`
processes); the catalogue's 91–103 °C launches "completed", they were not arithmetic-checked; the SRAM rail is 0.70–0.75 V
by card, not 0.705 V everywhere; the 75 °C PMIC alarm reads the PMIC's own temperature, not the die, and on 0.20.0 its
handler does not reprogram the PLL; the 52 °C in the firmware header is a default reset value, not an expected die
temperature; E53's two cards do not throttle at any temperature (aifoundry3 latched, card 1 on 0.18.0), so its mean-65
numbers are sensor statistics, and "nothing limits the die at 600 MHz" holds for the 0.20.0 and 0.18.0 cards, not for
card 0 (1.4.1), which dropped to 300 MHz after 115–117 °C.

Fixed upstream in `7c6049087` (2024-10-10), later than the lab's builds; 0.18.0 has no range check at all | R | et-platform `ffca4cbb4`, `da192816a`, `7c6049087` | `NV/DESIGN.md` §2, "The firmware's limits", and §9 |

## Sparse parity on the ET-SoC-1 (E59, 29 Sep; an engineering benchmark on one card, aifoundry3, not pre-registered)

Noisy sparse parity (x uniform in {±1}ⁿ, y the parity of a secret k-subset flipped with probability η), solved by an
exhaustive correlation scan written as an int8 tensor GEMM on 32 shires × 32 minions = 1,024 minions at 600 MHz. Every
card value below was **measured on one card** (aifoundry3, pinned at 600 MHz), seed 1, one launch per configuration
unless the row says otherwise. L1 = (512, 4, 0.3, 448), L2 = (512, 4, 0.4, 1,850), F5 = (256, 5, 0.4, 1,925), written
(n, k, η, m). `SPD` means `workloads/sparseparity/data/2026-09-29-aifoundry3-`, `SPCPU` means
`workloads/sparseparity/cpu/data/2026-09-29-aifoundry3`. A card time is the kernel launch waited for (one stage), or
that plus the survivors' readback and the host's stage 2 (two stages), without the device open (0.18 s); a CPU time is
its solve alone. The CPU is aifoundry3's host (i7-11700K), at most 6 threads.

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The sizes | L1 and L2: C(512, 4) = **2.83 × 10⁹** candidates; F5: C(256, 5) = **8.81 × 10⁹**. Noiseless parity is a GF(2) solve, n = 512 in **1.3 ms** on one core (aifoundry1's host), so only noisy instances need the chip | D; M (host CPU) | E59; SP3 | `workloads/sparseparity/proto/RESULTS.md`, "What we found" 1; `proto/data/2026-09-28-aifoundry1/` |
| M1's kernel on all 32 shires (M3, the first runs) | L1 **0.2045 s**, L2 **0.807 s**; the busiest minion 1,564–1,644 cycles per op, the slowest minion 1.56–1.64× the median | M | E59 (M3) | `SPD`card/`runs/m3-manual-0420/l1.log`, `l2.log`: `launch_s`, `kernel` |
| The best one-stage scan (variant b: the planner weighted by the fitted cycle model, incremental row generation) | L1 **0.130 s**, L2 **0.429 s**, F5 **2.55 s** in two halves (0.958 + 1.589 s); rerun in M5's session 0.135 and 0.427 s | M | E59 (M4, M5) | `SPD`card-m4/`sparseparity-m4.log` (`m4-32s-l1-b`, `m4-32s-l2-b`), `f5-b/h0.log`, `h1.log`; `SPD`m5-energy/`m5/m5-l1-b.json`, `m5-l2-b.json` |
| The epilogue split between the next tile's ops (variant c) | L1 0.203 s, L2 0.708 s: 1.4–1.8× slower than its fitted model and slower than b; with it, m4 0.198 and 0.581 s | M | E59 (M4) | `SPD`card-m4/`sparseparity-m4.log`, steps `m4-32s-*-c`, `-m4`, `-m4d` |
| The two-stage screen (stage 1 on the card at m1 samples, survivors rescored on the host on all m) | solve L1 **0.131 s** (m1 320, τ1 66, launch 0.125 s), L2 **0.323 s** (m1 1,152, τ1 106, launch 0.262 s), F5 **1.522 s** (m1 1,152, τ1 106, launch 1.335 s); survivors 376,740, 2,780,146 and 8,656,197; the secret survived and was the unique answer in all three | M | E59 (M5) | `SPD`m5-energy/`m5/m5-l1-s320.json`, `m5-l2-s1152.json`, `m5-f5-s1152.json`: `two_stage.solve_s`, `.found`, `launch_s` |

## The bit-flip ladder: what one flipped operand bit costs (E60, 20 Oct; pre-registered, three cards)

Added 26 October 2026 (Q126): between 14 October and this edit the index stopped at E59, while E60–E64 published
their own numbers. Board energy unless a row says the minion rail. `HL` means
`docs/reports/data/2026-10-20-bitflip-ladder/hl.json`; the ladder's slope is per **designed** flipped B mantissa bit
per multiply-add per minion, from the straight line through h ≥ 1, aifoundry3 pinned at 600 MHz and 0.522 V. The
pre-registration, the departures and the decision rules are [`tools/claims-v3/hl/README.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/hl/README.md).

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **One flipped operand bit of an fp32 multiply-add, quiet partner (A = 1.0)** | **116.7 ± 0.6 fJ** board, 87.5 fJ on the minion rail, the whole cascade included (predicted 122). | The same with a busy partner (A = π) | **129.9 ± 0.5 fJ** board, 96.9 rail (predicted 128) | M, F | E60 | same |

| Per counted RTL net toggle | 0.301 (quiet) and 0.119 fJ (busy) — 2.5× apart, so the toggle denominator is not settled; do not quote one of these as the price of a net toggle | D | E60 | same; the counts in `docs/reports/data/2026-10-20-bitflip-ladder/` |

## Verifying the deterministic transistor flips (E61, 21 Oct; pre-registered, three cards)

E61 re-measured E60's one-gate numbers in a separate build and scored fifteen registered rows: 1 CONFIRMED,
1 CONFIRMED-ours, **4 REFUTED**, 5 OPEN, 3 under-resolved, 1 not scored. `DTF` means
`docs/reports/data/2026-10-21-dtf/`; board energy on aifoundry3 at 600 MHz unless noted.

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A flipped result bit** | **29.67 ± 0.15 fJ** from the registered joint fit (26.7 ± 0.3 inside one kernel family). 🛑 **This supersedes the 44.4 fJ per result bit** published before E61, which E61 REFUTED; 44.4 fJ is dead and must not be quoted | M, F | E61 | `DTF/reduce.txt`; `tools/claims-v3/dtf/README.md` |
| An input bit plus an output bit (`fnot.pi`) | **50.7 ± 0.6 fJ**, so the old page's sum of 50.9 fJ holds — but its 6.81 : 1 split into input and output does not | M | E61 | same |
| **One carry position of the 32-bit adder** | **0.98 ± 0.80 fJ** (3.48 ± 0.74 with the changed-word term removed). 🛑 **This supersedes 14.2 fJ per carry**, REFUTED (CONFIRMED-ours); 14.2 fJ is dead | M, F | E61 | same |
| **`fmadd.ps` has no zero gate on the vector unit** | **0.23 ± 0.16 pJ per op**. 🛑 **This supersedes the 8.94 pJ zero gate**, REFUTED; 8.94 pJ is dead. Zero skipping still saves 82–97% on the tensor path (E39, the Sparse compute rows above) — the refuted number is the vector unit's gate, not the tensor unit's saving | M | E61 | same |
| The gate's own kind at equal flips (OR, ADD, XOR) | within 2.7 fJ of about 53 fJ, not ordered by depth: the gate is **≤ about 5%** of the cost of delivering a bit to it | M | E61 | same |
| One register-file cell | 0.76 ± 0.32 fJ — under-resolved, not a usable price | M | E61 | same |
| Changing which register is read | 369–452 fJ per index bit, 12–14× the page's 32 fJ, with no linear law over three matched-rate rungs. **An upper bound with a constant inside**; never quote it as the price of an address bit | M | E61 | same |
| The Miller patterns | monotone the wrong way (−0.22 ± 0.06 W over four k_adj levels): REFUTED as a delivered design | M | E61 | same |
| Two-hart interleaving | destroys operand sharing (ρ = 1.117 ± 0.024, aifoundry1 card 0) | M | E61 | same |
| The vector unit's issue rate | one op per cycle per hart; the harness's 0.58 / 0.61 ops a cycle is its loop overhead, set by the loop head's address mod 32 | M | E61 | same |
| Two independent pipelines over the same data | agree on 48 quantities within 1.6 SE | M | E61 | `DTF/independent/notes.md` |

## The camera half of the ladder: weighing a flip as heat (E63, 23 Oct; pre-registered, aifoundry2)

Two sessions, 256 and 192 minions, at 800 MHz and 0.618 V; the camera amplitudes are the 0.05 Hz component over
the die ± its SE over laps. `E63` means `docs/reports/data/2026-10-23-e63-camera-hamming-ladder/`; the five
registered rules and the full tables are [`tools/claims-v3/hlc/README.md`](../../tools/claims-v3/hlc/README.md).

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The flips' watts, top rung against its zero | **+5.443 ± 0.008 W** on 256 minions, **+4.020 ± 0.014 W** on 192; the middle rung +1.496 and +1.109 W; the null of equal watts −0.044 and −0.033 W | M | E63 | `E63/s*/analysis/lockin.json`, `blocks.*.board_step_w` |
| **A flipped bit weighed as heat, per designed flipped B bit** | **154.0 and 154.4 fJ** (session 1, 256 minions) and **151.7 and 152.4 fJ** (session 2, 192), the middle rung 121.7 and 120.3 fJ, at 0.618 V. The two sessions agree to 1.5%. Compare E60's 116.7 fJ at 0.522 V: × V² is ≈ 163 fJ, within about 5%. **Corrected 26 Oct 2026 (Q126)**: session 2 was published as 113.8 / 114.3 / 90.2 fJ, computed on 256 minions when it ran 192 | M, D | E63 | `E63/s*/summary.json`, `blocks.*.fj_per_flip` with `.minions` and `.mhz` |
| The back of the board follows the flips | **194.2 ± 9.1 mK** at 0.05 Hz over the die (256 minions; p at the 20,000-draw floor, SNR 21) and **118.7 ± 13.0 mK** (192); the null reads 1.9 ± 15.0 and −13.6 ± 8.4 along the top rung's phase | M | E63 | `E63/s*/analysis/lockin.json`, `blocks.*.roi` |
| **The board's thermal transfer at 0.05 Hz** | **59.0 ± 3.0 mK per watt** of fundamental at first, the heat **87 ± 5°** (about 5 s) behind the power. It **drifts**: 59.0 → 52.9 → 47.8 → 42.4 mK/W over the four top-rung blocks of the hour, the lag 87° → 109°. A camera wattmeter needs its own top rung near every reading | M, F | E63 | `E63/s*/analysis/lockin.json`, `blocks.*.transfer_roi` |
| The same transfer at 0.15 Hz (**post hoc, no verdict**) | **18.4 ± 5.4 mK/W** at 151° pooled over the four top-rung blocks against **52.7 ± 2.0** at 95° at 0.05 Hz, both vector means of the complex transfer; the ratio **0.34 ± 0.10**, which is **6.3 SE from flat**. The board rolls off between the two, as a single pole of **12 s** would, or any pole longer (1 σ: τ ≥ 3.2 s, with no upper bound, because a one-pole ratio bottoms out at 1/3 and the measurement sits on that floor), and the 87–109° lag at f is in any case more phase than one pole can give. **No top-rung block's camera third harmonic clears 2 SE** (0.8–1.5 SE each); the middle rung's 21.3 ± 9.8 mK is 2.2, and the board's own third harmonics are 4.3–14.3 SE. **Corrected 26 Oct 2026**: first published as 19.0 ± 5.2 against 54.1 ± 1.8, an inverse-variance mean of four *magnitudes*, which is biased upward, and "3.3 SE from flat", which was the ratio's distance from *zero* | M, F | E63 (Q126) | `E63/posthoc/h3-rails-pooled.json`, `pooled.H_3f_mk_per_w` and `distance_from_flat`; `E63/s*/posthoc/h3-rails.json`; [`tools/claims-v3/hlc/posthoc/`](../../tools/claims-v3/hlc/posthoc/README.md) |
| **Which rail the flips' watts land on** (post hoc, no verdict) | of each rung block's board step: the **minion rail 84.3–86.1%**, the SRAM rail 0.39–0.92%, the NoC rail 0.32–1.39%, the three together 85.3–88.3%. The missing 12–15% is the board's conversion loss and the rails the sampler does not break out | M, D | E63 (Q126) | `E63/s*/posthoc/h3-rails.json`, `blocks.*.steps` |
| The signal is heat on the board, not the camera | the in-phase map carries the capacitor field's emissivity pattern (high-passed correlation with E62's bloom map 0.90–0.92); the focal plane's thermistor shows nothing at f; the shutter fired 2.8–10.7 a minute at no preferred phase | M | E63 | `E63/s*/analysis/lockin.json`, `blocks.*.spatial`, `.fpa`, `.ffc` |
| 800 MHz, and what the launch records say | the median minion clock over the steady windows is **800 MHz in all eight blocks** (mean 797.4–800.0; the one-second dips pull it down). 🛑 `launches.jsonl`'s own `mhz_minion` is sampled before the launch opens and reads **600 MHz in all 360 records of both sessions** — never price a flip from it | M | E63 | `E63/s*/summary.json`, `blocks.*.clock`; `E63/s*/session/launches.jsonl` |
| The one-second clock dip | in 8 of 360 launches the clock fell 800 → 600 MHz for 0.6–1.5 s, 1.4–8.4 s into a launch, **at every load** (34.1–41.6 W in the six the sampler caught, two unrecorded) and a 54–57 °C die; the governor's idle-reset shape, not heat and not the 65 W level. It is what failed the hold gate on 256 minions (39 of 42 launches held, 92.9% against 95%) | M | E63 | `E63/dips.json`; `14-card-behaviour.md` |

R1 and R2 PASS | M | E64 | `docs/reports/data/2026-10-23-e64-horace-power-limit/README.md`, R1, R2 |

## Pricing a whole load: the joined model and its frozen predictions (Q126(c), 26 Oct; no new card time)

One model that joins the flip prices, the energy catalogue, a clock-and-voltage static term and the leakage
law into watts, a clock and a die temperature for a whole load. Four predictions were frozen by commit
(`92b30b8d`) before their measurements were opened, then scored. 🛑 **The freeze is by commit order, not
blind**: the session read the four experiments' records while choosing their operating points, and three
constants in the model come from those same held-out loads (`load_model.HELD_OUT_LEAKS` names them; E39's
own rail voltage made E39's worst number worse, not better). `LM` means
`docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-load-model/`.

| The same load on **watts**, which is what the model exists to predict | **+41.1 / +33.6 / +20.3%** on aifoundry2, decomposed into finding 18's own 17% internal inconsistency (+29.6 / +19.7 / +21.5 points), the +0.69 W static offset (+15.9 / +15.6 / +13.0) and the pJ/B error (−4.4 / −1.7 / −14.1) | P→M | Q126(c) | same, `loads.E43_relay.watts_decomposed` |

| E37, mmbench fp32 from DRAM | **2.14 W rms** at +22.7 / +24.0 / +27.0%; back-solving the byte count gives 1,577–1,604 B an op on three cards, agreeing to 2%, so one assumption carries the load | P→M | Q126(c) | same, `loads.E37_mmbench_fp32_from_DRAM` |

## One transistor flip from an open 7 nm kit (Q126(d), 26 Oct; simulated, no card)

ASAP7 is a predictive, academic 7 nm kit — the closest open stand-in for this chip's process, and **not**
its process; nothing here is a measurement of the ET-SoC-1. Part 1 reads its liberty, part 2 simulates one
switch in ngspice on ASAP7's own BSIM-CMG device cards. Internal energy is per output transition, averaged
over rise and fall and over each arc's mutually exclusive `when` states, **VDD group only**. `A7C` and `A7S`
mean `docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-asap7-cell-energies/` and `docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-asap7-one-switch/`.

## The multiply-add on an open synthesis flow (Track C, Q126(e), 26 Oct; simulated, no card)

core-et's `txfma_top` synthesised with Yosys onto ASAP7 and placed with OpenROAD, to get a physical net
count and a switching energy that do not depend on the RTL bench's counter. 🛑 Detailed routing does not
close in this OpenROAD, so **there is no SPEF**: every capacitance below is the global route's Steiner
estimate. `ON` means `docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-open-netlist/`; the flow is
`tools/open-flow/txfma/`.

🛑 **Superseded in part, Tue 27 Oct 2026 (Q129(a), `docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-q129-routed-netlist/`).**
That preamble used to end "the global route's Steiner estimate, **with no coupling term**", and the rows
below are written on that reading. Routing **does** close in a current OpenROAD (ORFS container, 0 DRC
violations on both netlists), the coupling **is** extracted, and the Steiner estimate was never
coupling-free: `setRC.tcl` hands it a layer's *total* capacitance at nominal spacing, so it sits **20–23%
above** the extraction at Miller k = 1. Every capacitance row below is therefore a **ceiling** on the wire,
not a floor, and re-pricing the identical SAIFs moves the wire between the cells 0.590 → **0.453 pJ**, the
bottom-up total 1.947 → **1.810 pJ**, and the closure against the board 33.7% → **31.3%**. The ladder
slopes move only −5 to −6%.

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **The multiplier's cells, like for like** | **4,989 cells and 42,758 transistors** on the comparable netlist (flattened except the four multiplier blocks) against the tape-out flow's 4,487 and 49,755 — **1.11× the cells, 0.86× the transistors**. | **Landauer's floor, at the die's own temperature** | **kT ln 2 = 3.246 zJ** at the governor's 66 °C trip. E60's own `reduce.txt` prints electrons as `C·V/e`, twice the convention used here | S | Q128(d) | `TL/transistor-ledger.json`, `convention` |
| **What the RTL bench's 838 register bits were** | the **83 instrumented library instances only** (`en_ff` 63, `rst_en_ff` 14, `rst_ff` 6, priced at 766 + 66 + 6 = 838 on the open netlist). **1,233 more bits** are declared with core-et's `FF macro family and are invisible to the bench; **838 + 1,233 = 2,071**, exactly the netlist's hierarchy-kept total and the chip diagram's own denominator. So `595.2/838 = 71%` is a fraction of the enable-gated subset, not the whole-unit pair for the netlist's 86% | S | Q128(e) | `docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-toggle-recount/README.md`; `ON/census_keep.json` |
| How many physical wires a flip really moves | the traced run's own VCD gives **7,232.7 RTL names** per random multiply-add; against the open netlist's **3,299 wires** that is **2.19 names a wire**, against the chip diagram's separately measured **2.55** — 14% apart, the best agreement any two routes reach. The merged-net floor of 1,301 would make a wire carry 5.56 names and is the outlier | S | Q128(e) | `docs/reports/data/2026-10-26-toggle-recount/verilator-5042-vcd.json` |
| **The page that joins all of it** | [From matmul speed to one transistor switch](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/et-soc1-matmul-to-transistor), space `5576af71-35da-4958-b437-aba8d59e2ee0`, public. Every figure on it is read at build time from one of the directories above | — | Q128(g), MT | `MT/README.md`; `MT/build_data.py` |

**Not established by Q128.** The governor's cap has never been moved, so step 1 of the chain is observational
(the knob test is the owner's call: the lab's rule sets no TDP, threshold or clock on a shared card). The
saturation law rests on seven in-sample rungs from one session, and the ladder in the *number* of
simultaneously flipping positions that would separate how-many from which-bits is built and unrun. E68's
registered scoring still uses the 1.508 W/°C slope; E66's measured slope is published beside it as a
sensitivity, not as a replacement. Half the design's capacitance is unpriced and the open flow had no SPEF,
no coupling term and no glitch measurement, so the bottom-up total is a floor and the transistor share a
ceiling — **amended Tue 27 Oct 2026 (Q129(a)): the SPEF and the coupling now exist, and they went the other
way.** The Steiner estimate was never coupling-free, so it was a ceiling on the wire: extracting it moves
0.137 pJ *out* of "accounted" (bottom-up 1.947 → 1.810 pJ, closure 33.7% → 31.3%) and the transistor share
is a floor, not a ceiling. What remains is the unpriced half (43.6% of the farads priced on a routed
design, slightly worse than 50.5%), the scope of one lane's arithmetic, and the glitch measurement — the
last unblocked but not made, an SDF with the extracted parasitics now existing for both netlists. Ones'
sustained clock is the chain's one real miss, 775 MHz predicted against 738 measured, which
wants a temperature term in the static-power surface. And the A side has no multi-bit ladder, so the
carry-over from one operand to the other is a choice between two defensible numbers that moves the
bottom-up prediction by 6%.

🛑 **Q129 answered every item of that paragraph, and not all of them the way it expected** (27–28 Oct 2026, next section): the cap and the threshold were moved, the ladder in the *number* of flipping positions was run, both cards' idle slopes were measured in the band each experiment ran in, the SPEF and the glitch measurement exist, the unpriced half is priced, the A side got its multi-bit ladder (and rejected the law), and the memory path and the trapping instructions were measured. What survived: the sustained gap is still a clamp, the A side's shape is still open, and the transport row that closes most of the gap is a model the card's own zeros stimulus contradicts.

## Q129: the governor moved, the wires routed, every farad priced, and the remainder at a fifth of what it was (27–28 Oct 2026; seven card experiments on aifoundry2 and aifoundry3, plus desk work)

Q128's own §5, answered item by item. Short names for the data directories, all under `docs/reports/data/`:
`E69` `2026-10-27-e69-governor-knob/`, `E70` `2026-10-27-e70-saturation-ladder/`, `E71`
`2026-10-27-e71-idle-slope-aifoundry3/`, `E72` `2026-10-27-e72-600mhz/`, `E73` `2026-10-27-e73-camera-frequencies/`,
`E74` `2026-10-28-e74-register-file-cells/`, `E75` `2026-10-28-e75-memory-flip-ladder/`, `E76`

`2026-10-28-q129-waterfall/`.

🛑 **Two things to carry with every figure below.** (1) **Two different closures against the board are published,
and a summary must say which it means:** the bottom-up rows alone, and those rows plus the measured board-to-rail
step's regulator account, which is a *named* account from a three-rail fit and **not** a bottom-up term. (2) **The
largest row added rests on a model the card's own zeros measurement contradicts** — one lane's transport model costs
0.654 pJ per MAC in the zeros stimulus where the whole board costs 0.27 pJ over idle — so a true accounting sits
**below** the bottom of every range quoted here.

### The ledger, rebuilt bottom up

| 🛑 **The wire between the cells goes the WRONG way when it is routed** | **0.590 → 0.453 pJ** at Miller k = 1 (band 0.209 at k = 0 to 0.696 at k = 2): **−0.137 pJ out of "accounted"**, because the global-route Steiner estimate was never a coupling-free floor — `setRC` hands it 0.1658 fF/µm, a layer's total capacitance with neighbour coupling bundled in and grounded — so it ran **20–23% ABOVE** the extraction | S | Q129, `RT` | `RT/README.md`; supersedes the "lower bound on every wire" reading |
| **The unpriced half of the design's capacitance, priced** | **+0.1007 pJ** per random multiply-add (switching +0.0802, internal +0.0205) — **not a doubling**. ### What Q129 refuted, superseded or withdrew

| Was | Now | Where |
|---|---|---|
| the minion rail ≈ 0.75 × board = **4.337 pJ** per random multiply-add | **measured 4.774 pJ**, band 4.549–4.951, ratio 0.825 [0.787–0.856] — inside Q128's own 0.70–0.89 spread | `RL` |
| the bottom-up ledger accounts for **1.946 pJ**, 33.7% of the board | **3.966–4.190 pJ**; **68.6–72.4%** of the board bottom-up, or **83.7–87.6%** with the regulators' row counted (say which); **83.1–87.8%** of the rail | `WF` |
| **unaccounted 3.837 pJ** against the board, 2.39 against the rail | **0.718–0.941 pJ** against the board (band 0.339–1.277) and **0.584–0.807** against the rail | `WF` |
| "the between-cell wire is a global-route estimate with **no coupling term**", i.e. a floor | the estimate **carries coupling already** and runs **20–23% above** the extraction, so that row was a **ceiling**: routing moves **−0.137 pJ out of "accounted"** | `RT` |
| **49.5% of the design's farads are unpriced**, worth perhaps a doubling | 100% priced, worth **+0.1007 pJ**; 43.1 of the 49.5 points carry **no switching energy at all**, and only 4.86% of the farads were ever made by CTS or `repair_design` | `UC` |
| "half the board is on no meter" / "4.5% of the board is on no meter"; the SRAM and NoC rails as per-MAC switching rows | **withdrawn**: their whole delta is their own leakage rise | `RL` |
| the saturation ladder's **`W∞` as a ceiling**, the "16.90 ± 0.20 W cap", "random data sits at 95% of the cap" | **withdrawn**: the data exceed both asymptotes; `W∞` is a curvature | E70 |
| a flipped bit's price "follows V² — confirmed" | the **rival (clock-scaling, n = 3.637) is refuted at ≥ 3.4 SE everywhere**, but the exponent is **bracketed 1.60–2.92**, and the ±10% test **fails at three of four matched slopes** | E72 |
| "the rail is slope-free, quote the rail" | **withdrawn**: the rail is uncorrected and moves 16% across the slope ladder | E72 |
| the registered **1.508 W/°C** leakage slope; E66's 0.2936 as a general card figure | 1.508 is **4–5×** every measured idle slope; a slope belongs to a **band** as much as to a card | E71, `LS` |
| aifoundry2 as **release 1.3.1 / BL2 0.20.0 / minion 0.23.0** | **release 1.4.0 / BL1–BL2 0.22.0 / PMIC 1.5.0 / minions 0.25.1**, read fresh 27 Oct 2026; the `0.20.0` in `et-lab-manifest` is the **host driver's** version | E69 |
| a TDP at or below a pattern's own power "traps the governor's power-down loop" (a 1.05 × TDP exit it can never meet) | **no such loop at BL2 0.22.0**: the check is one pass per DM-task pass, the guardband macros are unused, POWER_UP is always allowed — so a TDP of 0 is a **reversible pin**, released by writing the TDP back. 0.20.0's loop and aifoundry3's latch were read in that older source and are not contradicted; what is gone is the assumption that one build's mechanism describes the other's | E69, E72 |
| a raised cap "would be not interpretable" because a 16-bit power field **saturates** | the cast **wraps**, the field that narrowed was the **measured average** only, and the real failure mode is **less** throttling: above 65.536 W the power arm **inverts** and raises the clock over the cap. Answered for the card E69 used (0.22.0's locals are 32-bit) | `TW` |
| **E74's bound is in no JSON** (stated in an earlier handover) | `e0f8ecf8` put it in `rf.json` as `rule5_resolution.bound_95pct_fJ` | E74 |
| the unpriced-capacitance lane's "43.1 of 49.5 points carry no switching energy" read as **including** the renumbered buses | the buses are a **separate 3.98% / 598-net row worth +0.0857 pJ**, and they were **priced**, not empty | `UC` |

**Six things not to get wrong when re-using the ledger.** (1) **Do not add** the routed-wire −0.137 pJ or the
unpriced-capacitance +0.101 pJ to the 2.7055 pJ arithmetic row: the glitch pass prices the same farads at 100%
coverage and subsumes both. (2) **Do not apply the ×1.331 glitch factor to the transport row** — that netlist has no
route, no SDF and 37% of its capacitance priced. (3) **Do not multiply ×1.331 by the zero-delay figure** and expect
the arithmetic row: ×1.331 is the measured ratio of the **switching** term alone, the cells' internal energy is
**scaled** by the ×1.680 transition count, and the composite is ×1.406. (4) The two closures against the board
answer **different questions**, so 34% → 84–88% is not apples to apples; 34% → 69–72% is. (5) The closure's upper
half rests on a register-file **model** the card's own zeros measurement contradicts, so quote the range **with**
that sentence; the true figure is lower, and the top of the transport range is a ceiling for the shared-CTS-root
reason too. (6) **Leakage is not a row.**

**Not established by Q129.** The register-file array's **read ports, read mux tree and clocking** — the part the
card's own zeros stimulus contradicts — are still unpriced, and that is the single most valuable measurement left in
the waterfall; E74 bounds the **cell bit**, not the array. The **internal-energy half of the glitch step** (0.281 pJ
of a 5.783 pJ multiply-add) is disclosed but not measured: pricing a glitch on a cell *input* needs a flow that
prices internal energy per transition, which neither instrument in Q129 does. Two rows rest on a **correction**
rather than a measurement — the rail is measured at the launch *window* and corrected to the launch temperature, and
the choice between three leakage footings is worth 0.04 in the ratio and 0.23 pJ — so only a run launched and read
at **one** temperature would give it directly (**E77** is reserved for that run, which would also split the
regulators' 0.875 pJ row and needs a governor move under the field manual's gates). The **A side's** multi-bit shape
has no law. E72's exponent needs an idle leakage slope measured **at 800 MHz** and a rail-side idle slope, neither
of which exists for any card. The governor's **sustained model still lacks a thermal time constant** (both
duty-cycle predictions were 70 and 140 MHz low), γ and the thermal trip are jointly unidentified, and both sustained
`randn` cells and the lowered-threshold cell are **unmeasured or inconclusive**. E73's third, longer pole wants a
frequency **below 0.01 Hz**; its two unmeasured halves (G1's and G3's governor-log clauses in E72, the service
processor's trace ring) want a card re-run with a per-run ring dump, as E69's harness does. E75's **DRAM pattern**
question — a third image at the same counts but a longer period — has no experiment number yet, and its scratchpad
rung needs a kernel mode that copies the image in first. E76's static instruction counts were taken on another box.
And **a private-archive cross-check of the same multiply-add ran and is kept off this repository**, as Q128(c) was:
six verdicts, four of their arguments narrowed on review, the one publishable half committed here as `TW`.

## Q130: the kind of number — power and heat at a pinned clock, speed through the governor, for 51 kinds in three formats (31 Oct 2026; six card experiments on aifoundry3, aifoundry2 and aifoundry1's card 1, plus desk work)

The owner's ask: "fully explain the speed/heat/power issues of multiplying varying kinds of numbers" (Q130). Short
names for the data directories, all under `docs/reports/data/` unless a path is given: `E78`
`2026-10-31-e78-kinds-energy-matrix/`, `E79` `2026-10-31-e79-fp16-int8-bit-prices/`, `E80`
`2026-10-31-e80-kinds-speed/`, `E81` `2026-10-31-e81-dram-pattern/`, `E82` `2026-10-31-e82-kinds-aifoundry1/`, `E83`
`2026-10-31-e83-kinds-speed-rest/`; and the desk lanes `KS` `tools/claims-v3/kinds/` (the kinds spec, Q130(s); its
data record `2026-10-31-q130s-kinds/`), `INV` `2026-10-31-q130-kinds-inventory/` (Q130(a)), `RTL`
`2026-10-31-q130-rtl-formats/` (Q130(b)), `MOD` `2026-10-31-q130-kinds-model/` (Q130(c)), `GOV`
`2026-10-31-q130-governor-thermal-branch/` (Q130(d)), `LIT` `2026-10-31-q130-literature/` (Q130(e)), `ZG`
`2026-10-31-q130-zero-gate-correction/` (Q130(g)), `ZT` `2026-10-31-q130-zeros-transport/` (Q130(i)). Every lane was
re-derived by a separate adversarial verifier; the rows below carry the corrected figures, E83's from its correction
commit `734e997f`. E84 and E85, unverified or running when this section was written, are in "Q130 additions"
below, with the evening round's Q130(n) and E86–E91.

🛑 **Five things to carry with every figure below.** (1) **Name the meter, the zero a power is measured over and the
operating point:** every watt and pJ measured on these cards below is the **board** meter's unless a row names the
minion rail (E78 reports the rail, deconvolved, at 0.800–0.865 of board D, never scored); `D` is over the format's own all-zeros kernel, paired within the pass; `S` is over the run's own idle bracket;
E80's and E83's `c` is a fitted switching term at 600 MHz and 0.517 V over E64's per-clock baseline, not a power over
idle. E78 and E82 ran at a pinned 600 MHz; E80 and E83 under aifoundry2's governor at 600–800 MHz. (2) **Multiply-adds
per op depend on the format:** one 16 × 16 TensorFMA is 4,096 / 8,192 / 16,384 multiply-adds in fp32 / fp16 / int8,
so "MAC/s = ops/s × 4,096" is fp32's alone. (3) **Heat below about 12 W of switching is step-limited** by the die
reading's whole degrees — 32 of the 51 kinds, every int8 kind among them; S × 0.118 °C/W is an estimate there, not a
measurement. (4) **Every E78 and E82 interval is one fixed tile's repeatability** over passes at the primary leakage
slope; the registered leakage arms move fp32 random's D by +0.62 / −0.56 W (1.18 W between the two arms), and
re-drawing a kind's rule moves its gated fraction. (5) **Every RTL toggle count from Q130 is provisional**, and so is the toggle recount's: neither RTL bench
computes a·b+c on a multiply-add (fixed 1 Nov 2026, `rtl-sim/fma_toggle_v2/`: "Q132", below; these counts stay as they were). **How to read ±:** a ± or a bracketed range is a 95% interval over independent
passes unless the row labels it otherwise; the others are INV's standard errors, RTL's and E64's 1σ, KS's sd over 400
tile draws, GOV's band edges (the die line's rms residual), E83's one-run figures and E81's per-line spread (1σ),
E65's published SE, and a rule's registered tolerance (±0.30 W, ±0.35 W).

### What the RTL says about the three formats (Q130(s), Q130(b), Q130(g))

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **The operand stream is not the same in the three formats** | fp32 and fp16 (txfma): **512 micro-ops in 546 cycles**; the A word held two micro-ops and broadcast to 8 lanes, lane *l*'s B word alternating between columns *l* and *l* + 8 of one line. int8 (TIMA): **256 micro-ops in 318 cycles**, A broadcast to 16 column slots and changing every cycle, the B line held sixteen — so int8's operand bus is A-dominated | R | Q130(s) revision 2 | `KS/kinds.json`, `facts`; `external/core-et` `vpu_tensorfma.v:412`, `:717-718` |
| **One zero test, 32 bits wide, in every format** | the test is a whole 32-bit operand word; a gate decision needs **one word in fp32 and fp16 and two ANDed in int8** (columns *l* and *l* + 8: eight bytes), so at element sparsity *p* the gated rate is **~p / ~p² / ~p⁸** — never int8's word-zero rate p⁴. **Corrected 1 Nov 2026 (Q132(o)):** ~p⁸ is the B pair's rate; an A word gates a micro-op on its own four bytes, ~p⁴. With both operands sparse a lane gates at p⁴ + p⁸ − p¹², 6.6% at p = ½, the 6.7% below. E93 read both rules at power resolution | R | Q130(s), Q130(g) | `KS/kinds.json`, `facts`; `external/core-et` `vpu_ctrl.v:802-818`, `:1229-1234` |
| Expected gated lane-cycles | at 50% **element** sparsity: fp32 **75.0 ± 2.2%**, fp16 **43.7 ± 2.9%**, int8 **6.7 ± 1.5%** (± the sd over 400 draws; the committed tiles 77.4 / 41.4 / 7.0%); at 50% **word** sparsity 75.0 / 74.9 / 62.4% | S, D | Q130(s) | `KS/kinds.json`, `kinds.<type>.{sparse50,wsparse50}.replicates` |
| Ordering for the stream, not sorting the numbers | the stream-aware minimiser leaves **0.74 / 0.73 / 0.77** of the operand-bit flips; a naive value sort makes fp32 **worse** (1.025); a pairing-aware one 0.935. Upper bounds on what ordering can do (2-opt, greedy for int8's B side) | S | Q130(s) | `KS/kinds.json`, `sort_baselines` |
| **fp16 is arithmetically exact, and int8 is gated** | the PRM's fp16 "update only when all four operands are nonzero" is a documentation error (01-resources.md R1 already lists it) — the chip returns the plain sum from a packed pair with one zero half, at 50% and 100%, on three cards (the version-3 check's LAT-S1, E36). fp16 shares fp32's TXFMA datapath (masked into two corners of its Booth/Wallace array); int8 runs on separate TIMA units (four signed 9 × 9 products and a 32-bit add) gated by the same zero mask | R, M | Q130(g), Q130(b) | `ZG/README.md`; `RTL/README.md`; `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/results/lat.json`, LAT-S1 |
| A zero-gated lane's registers are clock-gated, in the floats | in fp32 and fp16 the lane's operand register files and pipeline stages sit behind explicit `et_clk_gate` cells enabled by its valid, which the zero mask removes; int8's are enable flops | R | Q130(b) | `RTL/README.md`; `external/core-et` `txfmafrac_top.v:337-356`, `vpu_ctrl.v:839`, `tima_top.v:63-66` |
| 🛑 **Neither RTL bench computes a·b+c on a multiply-add** | the formats bench: **0 of 4,080** fp32 MADD results right (3,740 equal c alone, 340 a·b alone); the older `rtl-sim/fma_toggle` bench, which feeds the toggle recount: **0 of 4,096** (3,755 c alone, 341 a·b alone; A = B = 1 with C0 = 5 leaves C = 5.0 against 37). Every toggle count from either is provisional until a bench passes `verification/fcheck.py`. **Fixed 1 Nov 2026 (Q132(b)):** the simulator's optimiser was the fault, and `-fno-dfg` the fix; what the fixed bench may and may not support is in "Q132", below | S | Q130(b) | `RTL/verification/fcheck.json`, `runs[new-*]`, `runs[old-fp32-*]` |

**Not established by Q130.** The zero gate's **width on silicon**: an all-zero tile is gated under any rule, so the
deciding run — a word with one zero element in each format, plus fp32 zeros — is open and needs a card. **int8's B
price list** (a bound at E71's slope), the int8 top bit's mechanism, and the 28-cycle difference between int8's and
the floats' overheads. **Heat for the 32 kinds below about 12 W of switching**, every int8 kind among them, which needs
a longer window or a finer reading. **The spread between tile draws**: every interval is one tile's repeatability.
**What moved aifoundry2's heat path** between 23 and 31 October (no ambient, inlet or fan reading exists). **A
successor power model** that passes the price-list rungs, fits E70's ladder, carries a mantissa-truncation term, the
gated-lane residual and an accumulator term — a new freeze, made before the data it is scored on. **The RTL toggle
counts**, until a bench passes the functional check. **The A side's broadcast weight**, which every A-against-B energy
claim rests on. **The idle reset's firmware cause.** The register file's **read ports, read mux tree and clocking** —
narrowed by Q130(i), not closed — and **E77**, the rail at one temperature, still reserved. **E84–E91 and Q130(n)**
are in "Q130 additions", below, which narrows two items of this list (heat below 12 W, E89; the spread between tile
draws, E90). And a **private-archive search** ran beside Q130 and is kept off this
repository, as Q128(c) and Q129's were; its one publishable half is `ZG`. **Since then, Q132 (1 Nov 2026, "Q132", below):**
the gate's width on silicon closes at power resolution on one card (E93); int8's B list is answered with A = 1 on one set of
base bytes (E102); the kinds below 12 W are measured on one card, and a settled plateau read, not a steady state (E97); the
draws' spread is measured with the draw as the unit (E94); the drift narrows, its cause unmeasured (E98); a successor model
narrows to this kernel (Q132(b), E103); the toggle counts exist on a fixed bench for fp32 and fp16 (Q132(b)); and the idle
reset is answered at the level of behaviour, on one card and one kind, not in the binary (E100).

## Q130 additions: a second card's governor, the camera per kind, real GPT-2 tensors, the result path, heat below 12 W, and signs (31 Oct 2026, afternoon and evening; eight card experiments on four cards, plus Q130(n) at the desk)

The owner's later asks of 31 October — *"feel free to use more cards"* (about 13:1x) and *"feel free to keep using the
lab"* (about 18:1x) — produced E84 and E85 in the afternoon and, from the Q130 critic's ranked list, Q130(n) and E86–E91
in the evening. Every lane was re-derived by a separate adversarial verifier and corrected in its own commit, and the
rows carry the corrected figures. Short names, all under `docs/reports/data/` unless a path is given: `E84`
`2026-10-31-e84-speed-aifoundry1-card0/`, `E85` `2026-10-31-e85-camera-kinds/`, `E86`
`2026-10-31-e86-real-tensors-energy/`, `E87` `2026-10-31-e87-real-tensors-speed/`, `E88` `2026-10-31-e88-result-path/`,
`E89` `2026-10-31-e89-heat-low-power/`, `E90` `2026-10-31-e90-signs-spread/`, `E91`
`2026-10-31-e91-real-tensors-aifoundry1/`, and `RT` `tools/claims-v3/kinds/real/` (Q130(n), the real tiles).
🛑 **The Q130 section's five things to carry hold here, and three more.** (6) **Every ratio names its baseline** — D over
the format's own zeros, S over idle, or the whole board — because int8's real-tile saving is 11–22%, 8–15% or 2–4% by
those three. (7) **The real tiles are three 16 × 16 windows of one model** (GPT-2 small: layer 0 MLP up, layer 6 MLP down,
layer 6 attention QKᵀ), int8 with one calibration: draws, not "real tensors" in general. (8) **E91 is not scored**: its
numbers are exploratory.

**Not established by the additions.** Whether card 0's PMIC alarm set point differs from aifoundry2's PMIC 1.5.0 or card
1's 1.3.0, and why the kernel's effective clock in the alarm cycle sits about 13% below the sampled one (E84). int8's
camera signal (not detected) and whether the camera's gain drifts within a session (E85). Whether the sampler's
statistics resets set off the clock excursions (E85, suggestive), and whether E85's resets left aifoundry2's SP
statistics trace off (unchecked). Real tensors beyond three windows of
one model, an activation with exact zeros (a ReLU network's), and any int8 real-against-random sustained difference (E86,
E87). fp16's +7.8 MHz burst gain (E87, 1.3σ). What random signs cost and where: E88 leaves 2.52 W with the result frozen
and E90 measures about 5 W for a random sign at a fixed magnitude; the adder alternating between add and subtract is the
registered guess, untested. The ten int8 and fifteen float kinds below 12 W that E89 did not run, and any steady-state
rise. The rules' means for E90's re-drawn rules, which need more draws. E91's replication, which needs a scored session,
and why its automatic leakage slope came out at 0.42 W/°C. **Since then, Q132 (1 Nov 2026, "Q132", below):** aifoundry2's
alarm is no longer untested, firmware or board unknown (Q132(g)); the statistics reset shows no rise in falls (E100); real
tensors beyond three windows and an activation with exact zeros are measured on one card each (E95, E96), and E96 reads real
int8 sustained near the cap; where a random sign's cost goes is narrowed to an estimate (E92, E104); the other kinds below
12 W are measured, and E97 reads a plateau; E94 measures the rules' means for E90's re-drawn rules.

## Q132: closing the kinds page's open items — signs, the zero gate, tile spread, more real tensors, heat below 12 W, the drift, the DRAM mechanism, the idle reset, a matmul that streams and its layout, and the bench's model (Sun 1 Nov 2026, week 44; fifteen card experiments on three cards, plus desk work)

The owner's ask, Sat 31 Oct 2026 about 23:30 PDT: *"Continue closing issues which are still open in
https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers . Update Horace Hub and run proper experiments on
aifoundry"* (Q132). Every card run was pre-registered and pushed before its first launch, and every lane was re-derived
by a separate verifier and corrected in its own commit; the rows carry the corrected figures, and a sentence a verifier
struck does not come back. Clocks went back at 02:00 PDT that night, so a time here says PDT or PST. Short names, all
under `docs/reports/data/` unless a path is given: `E92` `2026-11-01-e92-sign-split/`, `E93`
`2026-11-01-e93-zero-gate-silicon/`, `E94` `2026-11-01-e94-tile-spread/`, `E95` `2026-11-01-e95-real-tensors-energy/`,
`E96` `2026-11-01-e96-real-tensors-speed/`, `E97` `2026-11-01-e97-heat-low-power-steady/`, `E98`
`2026-11-01-e98-heat-path-drift/`, `E99` `2026-11-01-e99-dram-mechanism/`, `E100` `2026-11-01-e100-idle-reset/`, `E101`
`2026-11-01-e101-real-matmul/`, `E102` `2026-11-01-e102-balanced-bit-prices/`, `E103`
`2026-11-01-e103-model-blind-test/`, `E104` `2026-11-01-e104-c-readback/`, `E105` `2026-11-01-e105-gemm-layout/`, `E106`
`2026-11-01-e106-held-signs/`, `QB` `2026-11-01-q132-rtl-bench/` (Q132(b)), `QG` `2026-11-01-q132-governor-desk/` (Q132(g)),
and `RT2` `tools/claims-v3/kinds/real2/` (Q132(r), the second real-tile set). "Item N" here is the list the kinds page carried until
31 October; its rebuild, now `5450fa1ebd61`, ranks section 11 afresh and prints each item's "old item N".
🛑 **The Q130 section's things to carry hold here, and four more.** (9) **Each result is one card's,** board power at
600 MHz unless a row says otherwise, on the card its row names. (10) **Every per-kind number outside E101 and E105 is the resident
loop's:** one 16 × 16 TensorFMA repeating on tiles held in the minions. (11) **"Frozen" in fp16 is E104's word to give:**
never say it of fp16's `h_sub`, `c_alt`, `hh_sub`, `hl_sub`, `s_abs`, `h_allsub` or E88's `r_abs`. (12) **Each data
README's lists of what may and may not be said bind a quotation:** read them first.

| **The script's format has a source, and the pair's does not** | the post names no format anywhere, and the pair's snippet has a bare `N`, though the runs earlier in the same story are 8192 × 8192 × 8192. Its recorded runs read zeros/randn 292.30 / 239.41 and 291.63 / 240.43, **×1.21–1.22**, and the post's chart from pixels 287.69 / 236.47, ×1.22, against the text's 295 / 257, **×1.148**, told in a passage that opens "It's 2022". **Not established by Q132.** **Where a large GEMM falls,** on this chip or on a GPU: E105 reads three conditions and places none (a sweep of block repeats; 8 blocks, 24 MB of A + B, which overflow the L2 and fit the L3; the vendor's binary, untimed). What limits a fresh-block walk. Whether reuse itself is the variable. **Sustained random fp32 by column, which could not be expected to complete under the 97 W stop on aifoundry2,** and the benchmark's random fp32 and fp16 speed (E101, E105). **Where a random sign's cost goes:** on one card a bracket by held tile; the rest is by subtraction and not located; `signs` itself cannot be held; the moving-sum ratio is unresolved (E104-R6, E106). **Real tensors in int8:** bits against the
gate, and speed at the trip's edge (E95, E96), and anything beyond one card each. **The heat path's cause:** no inlet
thermometer; a probe at E64's own idle die on a warm day; more than one warm probe (E98). **A replicated plateau,** on
another card, and for fp32 random at full duty, and E95's tiles below 12 W (E97). **The idle reset in the card's binary,**
per card and per kind (E100). **The alarm's reason:** firmware or board, and the slow average, which needs a governor move
or a PMIC read (Q132(g)). **The 1.3 pJ of a one off every metered rail, what carries `half` and the 64-bit toggles, and what
sets the ladder's steps** (E99). **fp32 A's low bits, the sign's rail leg, and the other 22 positions** (E102). **A model
past this kernel:** fp32 where signs alternate on moving sums, the A side with a busy partner, gated kinds, int8 across
formats, other clocks (E103). **Why a live zero on A costs 1.72 W more than priced, and the mask tile's +2.2 W** (E93).
**int8 zeros' 0.61 W, zeros' transport, and Q129's waterfall,** which has not been rebuilt. **A GPU measured the same way,**
which was not in this run.

## Q133: the Esperanto side's missing parts — a large GEMM's placement, a sign's rest located, int8 zeros, the memory path's width, a steady coefficient, replicates on second cards, the A side's form, the power model's blind pool, int8 at gate level, and E72 on a second card (Sun 1 Nov 2026, week 44; eleven card experiments on four cards and one not run, plus desk work)

The owner's ask, Sun 1 Nov 2026 about 09:50 PST: *"continue filling out the missing parts (Esperanto only) found by the
critics"* (Q133), with a note that another session (Q132) was working on similar tasks; about 12:27, his OK for governor
moves. It takes the kinds page's section 11 as Q132 rebuilt it
([what is still open](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open), the list at
`1fe8377a18af`, which the Horace hub's Status quotes) and the final critic's replicates. Every card run was pre-registered and
pushed before its claim, and every lane was re-derived by a separate verifier and corrected in its own commit; the rows carry
the corrected figures, and a sentence a verifier struck does not come back. Short names, all under `docs/reports/data/` unless
a path is given: `E107` `2026-11-01-e107-gemm-placement/`, `E108` `2026-11-01-e108-sign-located/`, `E109`
`2026-11-01-e109-int8-zeros/`, `E110` `2026-11-01-e110-memory-width/`, `E111` `2026-11-01-e111-heat-steady/`, `E112`
`2026-11-01-e112-real-replicates/`, `E113` `2026-11-01-e113-a-side-saturation/`, `E114` `2026-11-01-e114-card0-replicates/`,
`E115` `2026-11-01-e115-blind-pool/`, `E116` `2026-11-01-e116-split-replicate/`, `E118` `2026-11-01-e118-two-clock-card0/`,
`QA` `2026-11-01-q133a-int8-gate-level/` (Q133(a)), `QM` `2026-11-01-q133b-power-model/` (Q133(b)), `B3`
`tools/claims-v3/kinds/blind3/` (blind pool 3), and `QG` and `QB` as in the Q132 section. "Item N" is that list's. E77, run at
the end of the day under the same governor OK, was being corrected after its verification when this section was
written; an addendum records it (dated note, 1 Nov 2026: "Q134", below). **How to read ±:** as in the Q130 section, a ± or a bracketed range is a 95% interval
over independent passes unless the row says otherwise, and a rule's band (±0.30 W and the like) is its registered
tolerance. The exceptions are standard errors: E113's figures (and E70's ρ values beside them) and E118's (and E60's
102.49 ± 4.42 fJ beside them), wherever they appear in this section; E111's camera figures (the lag's by jackknife over
laps; E73's beside them are standard errors too); and Q133(a)'s fitted constants (jackknife over kinds), as M5's adder
difference says of itself (1 s.e.). E114's climb, 0.39 ± 0.05 s, is a median and its standard deviation.
🛑 **The things to carry from the Q130 and Q132 sections hold here, and three more.** (13) **A verdict names its reading.**
Where the registered primary scored nothing, a row says which registered fallback it reads (E115's dips-removed arm), or says
NOT SCORED and labels its readings (E109, E112(b)). (14) **An RTL-side count names its merge,** `nets_map` (Q132(b)'s primary)
or `nets_hier`: at RTL they differ, at gate level they agree. (15) **A price on a desk count is post hoc:** E107's prices per
missing line rest on a desk L2 model's line counts and E109's prices per clock edge on the RTL bench's register count, and
that RTL is core-et, a later revision (Erbium), not the ET-SoC-1's own.

**Not run: E117, aifoundry2's alarm by a governor move.** It would test item 14, whether aifoundry2's PMIC has card 0's 75 W
alarm on its slow average. The desk's NO-GO stands until the owner accepts an outage risk he has not yet been asked about, and
the desk recommends a skip: the run would hold aifoundry2's governor average at the PMIC's 75 W set point, and the public
firmware source lists an over-power power cycle among the PMIC's reset causes (et-platform `836a4ab`,
`device-bootloaders/src/ServiceProcessorBL2/include/pmic_hal.h:62`), though no lab card has been seen to take that path (card 0
alarmed 177 times and stayed up; aifoundry2 read up to 101.15 W and stayed up; `QG` §a). Item 14 keeps its text.
*(Dated note, 2 Nov 2026, Q134's records after their verification, the text above unedited: "he has not yet been asked
about" did not hold. The session had put E117's outage risk to the owner at 16:04 PST on 1 November, before this was
written, and put it again at 18:57 (the [Horace hub](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-hub)'s record). E117 stays
not run, awaiting his acceptance. Item 14 has since been narrowed at the desk by Q134(a), "Q134" below.)*

|---|---|---|

| (the kinds page's item 1; E101's registration) the vendor's binary, "about 58 µs a launch" | the FMA-bound desk figure, not a timing; E107's labelled prediction 85–98 µs at 600 MHz; an instrumented build is possible from public sources, not made | E107 |
| (E107's first version) "DRAM sets most of a fresh walk's pace"; 4 ns per L3 line alone | a share of the cold walk's extra time over a warm block, by format; 4.15 ns at the launch's pace with 2.8 ns on the mean minion beside it, both on a desk count | E107 (C1, C2) |

**Not established by Q133.** **Where a large GEMM falls:** it needs its own reuse per L2-sized block and its footprint, C
included, the speed half on a governed card, and the vendor's binary timed (E107). **Random fp32 and fp16 in the benchmark,
and sustained random fp32 by column** (item 4), not touched. **Where a random sign's ~5 W goes:** in fp32, how much goes with
the sums changing; fp16's rest; the fp16/fp32 moving-sum ratio (1.614 ± 0.480, unresolved) (E108). **int8 zeros:** a scored
replicate (4–5 passes); where int8's 1.7 W with nothing issued goes; random B inside gated lanes (E109). **The memory path:**
the split of a one's ≥ 1.0 pJ beyond the regulators, where the 256-bit path is, the encoded-side residual, each rail's period
steps (E110). **Heat:** an inlet thermometer; more than one warm probe and one at 23 October's idle die; a hold that passes
the registered steadiness test; the camera's lag at 0.005 Hz, which needs at least ten laps (E111). **Real tensors:** E95's
tiles on a second card (E112(b)); card 1's automatic leakage slope, 1.7× E82's, unexplained. **The A side:** the busy partner,
other base words, fp16 and int8, a gated lane, the top rung's miss and the low rungs (E113). **Card 0:** the variation between
sessions, and its fp32 sustained cells and int8 `neg_ones` (E114). **The power model:** the float B-lane gate's cause; A held
constant down columns; fp16 cancelling across k-groups; int8's 0.20 W bias; idle; the A side on the single-bit ladders; other
clocks, voltages and streams; a new card without a scale; a pool written by someone else; M5 against M4 (E115). **E88's
split:** why the parts scale unlike between the cards, and random fp32's 10% the split does not place (E116). **int8 at gate
level:** which multiplier the chip has; glitch counts; the sign bit's double price; the clock term that separates int8 from the
floats (Q133(a)). **E72's exponent** on any card (E118). **aifoundry2's alarm** (E117 not run). **E77's result,** in the
addendum after its verification. *(Dated note, 1 Nov 2026: recorded in "Q134", below, beside E120, its redesign;
"Q134" also takes up a scored int8-zeros replicate (E124), the A side on a second card (E125), E108's and E93's designs on
aifoundry3 (E122, E121), the speed half in seven of E107's arms (E119) and item 14 at the desk (Q134(a)).)* **A GPU measured the same way, and Horace He's pair,** which "Esperanto only" left out.

## Q134: confirmations while the cards were free — a large GEMM's speed half, E77 and its redesign, the zero gate, signs, tile spread, int8 zeros and the A side on second cards, and aifoundry2's alarm read from its own counter (Sun 1 Nov 2026, week 44, evening; seven card experiments on three cards, one of them a governor move, plus a desk lane)

The owner's ask, Sun 1 Nov 2026 about 19:05 PST: *"Feel free to run more experiments to do confirm the code or the cards are
available also"* (Q134; read as: run more experiments to confirm results while the cards are free), with his rule of one card
post a day, rewritten at each reservation and release. It takes the kinds page's section 11 as Q133's rebuild left it ([what
is still open](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open), live as `fdcbcba2f34d`, its
numbering `1fe8377a18af`'s) and E77's open issues. The page itself took Q134 before these records were committed: Q134(d)
rebuilt it in place as `424503b97c24` (22:57:31 PST) and, after its verifier, as `fbb682f9393b` (23:33:25 PST), keeping
that numbering and adding item 16, the rail ledger's off-rail row (E77, E120). Every card run was pre-registered and
pushed before its claim, and every
lane was re-derived by a separate verifier (all eight returned holds with fixes) and corrected in its own commit; the rows
carry the corrected figures, and a sentence a verifier struck does not come back. E77, run at the end of Q133's day and
corrected after its verification (`4bb512f1`, `70f1e13a`), is recorded here beside its redesign, E120. Short names, all under
`docs/reports/data/`: `E119` `2026-11-01-e119-gemm-speed/`, `E120` `2026-11-01-e120-rail-redesign/`, `E121`
`2026-11-01-e121-zero-gate-replicate/`, `E122` `2026-11-01-e122-signs-replicate/`, `E123`
`2026-11-01-e123-tile-spread-rest/`, `E124` `2026-11-01-e124-int8-zeros-replicate/`, `E125`
`2026-11-01-e125-a-side-replicate/`, `QR` `2026-11-01-q134a-alarm-residency/` (Q134(a)), `E77`
`2026-11-01-e77-rail-one-temperature/`, and the Q133 section's names. "Item N" is section 11's. **How to read ±:** a ± or a
bracketed range is a 95% interval over independent passes unless the row says otherwise, and a rule's band is its registered
tolerance. The exceptions: E125's figures are standard errors, as E113's are; E77's and E120's R_eff and their bounds are 99%
intervals over passes, as registered, and so are E120's T1, S1 and S2 and E77's post hoc ratio and steps (each of those
rows says 99%); E77's board drift is ± one standard error over its 43 body windows; E123's unit is the draw (a kind's mean
over draws, the SD pooled over a class of rows, as registered). 🛑 **The things to carry from the Q130, Q132 and Q133
sections hold here, and three more.** (16) **A replicate
names both cards,** and gives each card's figure beside the other's: "the second card" depends on which card ran first
(aifoundry3 for E78's matrix, E109 and E113; aifoundry1's card 1 for E93 and E108). (17) **Levels over idle can carry a
session offset** (E124: 0.10–0.28 W below E82 and E93 on the same card while D ties): compare sessions on differences (D, G,
N), never on S alone. (18) **Raw residency-state numbers depend on the build** (on BL2 0.22.0 IDLE 0, UP 1, DOWN 2, SAFE 3;
on 0.18.0 and 0.20.0 POWER_SAFE is raw 5), and a read of a state the build does not have logs two `SP Runtime Error` events
(E77 F1).

Post hoc (CV1): the row's 0.188 W/W is a fit on uncorrected over-idle deltas, and on E120's drift-corrected footing the off-rail that grows with the minion rail is 0.081 and 0.099 W/W in the two arms (0.38–0.46 pJ), less than half the row; so the upper edge is more than twice all the off-rail arm A measured at its top rung, and the row's remainder is not shown to be logic in step. ### Two disclosures about PMIC access (found at the desk before the governor moves of 1 November)

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🛑 **The firmware-revisions query is a PMIC command, and the lab sent it as a read since September** | `DM_CMD_GET_MODULE_FIRMWARE_REVISIONS` writes the PMIC's firmware-management command register twice, ACTIVE_SLOT then VERSION, at et-platform `836a4ab` (E114's C1), a PMIC command by E85 C1's standard. Sent as a read-only identity read by E69 (27 Oct: one at 13:56:26 PDT, and its records say the identity was re-read after the last run; no committed file keeps either output), E72 (27 Oct: 14:59:39 PDT in its governor log, and a re-read at 15:24:48 that its change record states and no file keeps), E79, E82 and E84 (31 Oct: 09:27:22, 13:45:24 and 15:42:00 PDT, under Q130's "no PMIC work"), E114 (1 Nov, already disclosed), the version-3 check (25–26 September, one a pass on each of three cards, nine in all, `tools/claims-v3/tel/block.sh`) and the aifoundry1 fix of 25 September (its transcript); the cards answered normally each time. Dated notes in the E69, E72, E79, E82 and E84 data READMEs say so; identity now comes from uptime continuity (E77, E118, E120) | R, M | Q134 records; E114 (C1) | `docs/reports/data/2026-11-01-e114-card0-replicates/README.md` C1; `docs/reports/data/2026-10-27-e72-600mhz/gov.jsonl` (the `identity` event); `tools/claims-v3/lq6/CHANGE-RECORD.md`, "Final handoff"; `tools/claims-v3/knob/CHANGE-RECORD.md`, "Firmware identities"; `docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e79-fp16-int8-bit-prices/ident/firmware.txt`; `docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e82-kinds-aifoundry1/ident/firmware_pre.txt`; `docs/reports/data/2026-10-31-e84-speed-aifoundry1-card0/ident/ident-154200.out`; `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-claims-v3/raw/*/tel/p*/gov/fw.txt`; `docs/reports/data/2026-09-25-aifoundry1/fix-transcript.txt` |
| **The stock telemetry reads PMIC registers: an open question, not a violation** | `ettelem config` sends `GET_ASIC_VOLTAGE` (two PMIC voltage-register reads a call), and each `ettelem sample` reading sends it and `GET_MODULE_VOLTAGE` (eleven a sample): reads with no write, in every run since September. Whether "no PMIC command" covers such reads is **an open question for the owner and Roman, the lab's firmware maintainer**. The governor moves of 1 November (E77, E118, E120) used a build with both reads off; E119 and E121–E125 used the stock build and disclose its reads. *Dated note, Mon 2 Nov 2026 (Q136's records): answered — the owner's ruling of 2 November 2026 allows read-only PMIC voltage-register reads ([AGENT.md](../../AGENT.md) §5; "Q136", below)* | R | Q134 records; E118 | `docs/reports/data/2026-11-01-e118-two-clock-card0/CHANGE-RECORD.md`, "PMIC voltage-register reads, no write"; the Q134 READMEs' departures |

### What Q134 refuted, superseded or withdrew

| Was | Now | Where |

**Not established by Q134.** **A large GEMM:** the clock gap in `c4s`, `c8` and `c16s`, a large GEMM itself, and the vendor's
binary timed; a replicate of any governed clock or gap (E119 ran one run per cell). **The regulator split:** a bound tight
enough to matter, which needs more passes, a registered screen on time at 800 MHz, an anchor for the logic-in-step
coefficient and another governor move; whether the ledger's 0.875 pJ row should be re-cut on a drift-corrected footing
(E120). **Signs:** how much of fp32's rest goes with the sums changing; fp16's rest; the moving-sum ratio (E122). **Tile
spread:** fp16 50% zeros and the two int8 90% rows, which need more draws; E94's translated rows on aifoundry3 (E123). **int8
zeros:** where the 1.6–1.7 W with nothing issued goes; R1 needs more passes or a third card; the session offset in S (E124).
**The A side:** the busy partner, other base words, fp16 and int8, a gated lane, the top rung's 0.6–0.7 W miss on both cards,
and whether E70's small-k B rungs read low (E125). **Item 15:** why a live zero on A costs more than priced and the
half-gated int8 tile's extra; the micro-op counters (E121). **aifoundry2's alarm:** an alarm on the slow average, which needs
a governor move that lets the average pass 75 W (E117's design, not run, awaiting the owner's acceptance of a possible
outage) or a PMIC read; firmware or board; the build hash; the set point (Q134(a)). **The PMIC register reads:** the owner's
and Roman's answer. **A GPU and Horace He's pair**, outside this round as in Q133.
*Dated note, Mon 2 Nov 2026 (Q136's records): the re-cut of the 0.875 pJ row is done (Q136(b): it stands at 0.75–1.05 pJ),
and the PMIC register reads are answered by the owner's ruling of 2 November 2026, which allows read-only PMIC voltage-register reads;
"Q136", below, carries what Q136 settled of the rest.*

## Q136: the owner's three rules on device commands, and nine card experiments under them — E107's last arms and a governed replicate, the float B-lane gate, fp16's sign rest, int8's no-issue excess with one A row, which int8 multiplier, E95's tiles on a second card, the memory path's split, heat holds again and more single-bit prices; at the desk, the write-command audit, frozen netlist predictions, the off-rail row re-cut and the TIMA bench (Mon 2 Nov 2026, week 45; nine card experiments on three cards, no WRITE command sent)

The owner's words, Mon 2 Nov 2026 shortly after 06:30 PST: *"continue the experiments but don't do any power cycles, read only
PMIC voltage reads should be fine, take more care for writing commands"* (Q136), which [AGENT.md](../../AGENT.md) §5 carries
as "Device commands: the owner's three rules of 2 November 2026". The round took the kinds page's section 11 as Q134 left it
([what is still open](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open), live as
`fbb682f9393b`) and Q134's final critic's ranked list. The page itself took Q136 before these records were committed: Q136(p)
rebuilt it in place as `a018f485d20b` (14:07:15 PST) and `2d0d2df10c44` (14:10:15), keeping that numbering. Every card run was
pre-registered and pushed before its claim, every lane was re-derived by a separate verifier (each returned holds with fixes)
and corrected in its own commit, and the rows carry the corrected figures; a sentence a verifier struck does not come back.
E133's second session was verified after this section was first written (holds with fixes; corrections `f50c77fd`, wording and
dated notes only), and its rows below carry the verified reading (dated, Mon 2 Nov 2026). E135, the GPU measured the same way
under the owner's OK of the same morning, is recorded on its own. Short names, all under `docs/reports/data/`: `E126`
`2026-11-02-e126-gemm-arms/`, `E127` `2026-11-02-e127-b-gate/`, `E128` `2026-11-02-e128-fp16-signs/`, `E129`
`2026-11-02-e129-int8-noissue/`, `E130` `2026-11-02-e130-int8-netlists/`, `E131` `2026-11-02-e131-real-tiles-card1/`, `E132`
`2026-11-02-e132-memory-split/`, `E133` `2026-11-02-e133-heat-holds/`, `E134` `2026-11-02-e134-bit-prices-more/`, `QW`
`2026-11-02-q136w-write-audit/` (Q136(w)), `QA` `2026-11-02-q136a-int8-netlist-predictions/` (Q136(a)), `QB`
`2026-11-02-q136b-rail-row/` (Q136(b)), `QC` `2026-11-02-q136c-tima-bench/` (Q136(c)), and the earlier sections' names. "Item N"
is section 11's. **How to read ±:** a ± or a bracketed range is a 95% interval over independent passes (over blocks in E130 and
E134) unless the row says otherwise; E128 has two passes (t = 12.71) and E129 three (t = 4.303). The exceptions: E126's fit
intervals are within its session; E133's c and R1 brackets are ranges, one hold per kind (its README's [range]); Q136(b)'s
re-cut intervals are approximate 95% (1.96 on a pass s.e. from three or five passes) and its conduction intervals 99% over R;
Q136(c)'s ranges run across the bench's constant sets and are not intervals, and its ± is a 95% t interval over the cards'
passes; Q136(a)'s margins are in card 95% half-widths. 🛑 **The things to carry from the earlier sections hold here, and four
more.** (19) **A GET can write, and a stop field named PMIC may not read the PMIC:** classify every command from the source of
the build the card reports (Q136(w)), and read `temp_c.pmic` as the shire mean (E127 C5). (20) **A governed sustained cell is a
window of a warm-up:** quote it with its session, the idle before it and the run's start temperature (E126). (21) **A frozen
expectation is not a rule:** E127's ALL-OFF and LONG and E130's BOOTH ON B failed as expectations and are reported so. (22)
**The off-rail row's coefficient is a leakage-corrected fit,** never an uncorrected one (Q136(b)).
