**Not established by Q136.** **A large GEMM** itself (its reuse per L2-sized block and its footprint, C included) and the
vendor's binary timed; a governed sustained gap that holds across sessions (E126). **The B-lane gate:** what spends a flip's
price, whether it is linear in flips, how lane position enters, the A-side gate at equal activity, int8's gate, and an M5 refit
scored on unseen tiles (E127). **Signs:** the part of the rest that goes with the sums changing, which needs more passes of
E128's matched tiles, and fp16 on the matched design (E128). **int8's no-issue excess:** which event carries which part (per
cycle, TenC's read port, the TIMA controls or the core; per row, the address walk or the A path), and E129's own R1 at six
passes (E129, Q136(c)). **Which multiplier:** Booth on B against none, which needs the rail as the primary or many more B blocks
(E130), and booth against booth_tree, which needs a glitch-aware count (Q136(a)). **Real tensors:** real int8 on a warmer heat
path, real int8 beyond GPT-2's three tiles, an int8 calibration's clock, no-zero tiles sustained, and E95's tiles' heat below
12 W (E131). **The memory path:** the 1.0 pJ of a one between the shires' logic, the PHY and the DRAM; where the 256-bit path
sits; the encoded part's two verdicts and its board cost on a footing the leakage slope does not move (E132). **Heat:** a hold
steady by the registered test, and the inlet (E133). **Single bits:** B bits 1–5, 17–19, 21, 24–26 and 28–29; A above bit 7;
more words; the sign on the rail; E102's bit-22 cell on a second card (E134). **The off-rail row:** the conduction term
registered (E120 again with eight or more passes, its own drift as the central footing, one form for the term fixed first and a
screen on time at 800 MHz), which needs a governor move, and the rest of the row split between the regulators' other losses and
logic in step (Q136(b)). **Writes:** command 74's class, which needs the maintainer's patch source; whether a card still sits
at the SP log level an earlier run set; the running binaries' hashes (Q136(w)). **Not in this round:** governor moves,
aifoundry2's alarm (item 14), E117, item 4 and Horace He's pair.

## E135: a GPU measured the same way — Horace He's kinds of numbers on two A100s, clocks, power and the power controller's reasons logged per kind (Mon 2 Nov 2026, week 45, under Q136 at the owner's OK; on Modal, no lab card)

The owner's OK, Mon 2 Nov 2026 about 10:23 PST, recorded in Q136's row of [02-requests.md](02-requests.md): *"btw you can do 'gpu measured the same way' by using modal, not exceeding
$10 in experiments (use A100 or cheaper)"*. It answers item 2 of the kinds page's section 11, "A GPU measured the same way"
([what is still open](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open)), which until now
rested on the literature (Q130(e)). Pre-registered (`89378ba6`, pushed before any GPU call; amendment A1 `73fc73a7` after
the declared smoke), run on two A100-SXM4-40GB boards at their default 400 W limit (units A and B, one session), reduced by
the registered code (`ffbcd3d8`), and re-derived by a separate verifier, whose faults were all corrected (`0e7cc89d`,
`57809cdc`, C1–C13; no frozen file, data, derived file or verdict changed). The rows carry the corrected figures. Short
name, under `docs/reports/data/`: `GPU` `2026-11-02-e135-a100-kinds/`; the registration is `tools/gpu/e135/`. **How to read
±:** bf16's sustained gap carries two 95% intervals, ±0.006 over its four passes (which share two boards) and ±0.038 over the
two boards' means; the other formats ran one pass per board, and their ranges are the two boards'. **Post hoc** rows rest on
a power law fitted after the data on one to three pre-boost readings per kind from a power reading that updates every
0.1–0.2 s; they carry no verdict. 🛑 **To carry from here:** (a) **an ET-SoC-1 figure set beside an A100 figure names its
kernel** (the resident loop, E105's repeated GEMM-pattern block, E119's and E126's walks): the A100 ran a large streaming
cuBLAS GEMM and the ET-SoC-1 has run none, so neither a gap's size nor zeros' share of random's switching separates the
chips; what differs by chip is the limit and the controller. (b) **"The cap is all that sets it"** holds on these two boards at
400 W, 8192³, square K-contiguous operands. (c) **No claim at 330 W is measured.**

### Section 11, item 2, after E135

| Item | After E135 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 2. A GPU measured the same way (old item 5) | **narrowed, not closed:** measured at the default cap on two A100-SXM4-40GB boards, burst and sustained, with clocks, power, temperatures and the clock-event reasons logged per kind every 100 ms; the size ladder answers the size half of the layout question on one board, one run per size. Still open: a lower cap on the same board (refused in Modal's container; it needs a host with root on the GPU), his board and an H100, shapes other than square and K-contiguous, and a large streaming GEMM on the ET-SoC-1 to set beside it (E105, E107 and E119 run none) | [E135](../reports/data/2026-11-02-e135-a100-kinds/README.md) |
| 3. Horace He's pair, like for like | unchanged, a question for him; his loop's zeros reproduce on these boards (below) | — |

**Not established by E135.** **A lower cap** on the same board, and so R5 and anything at 330 W (it needs a host where
`nvidia-smi -pl` is allowed). **Why his loop reads less** than the sustained cells (post hoc readings only). **His board, an
H100, other shapes** (only square, K-contiguous operands ran), and a replicate of the size ladder (one run per size, one
board). **A like-for-like ET-SoC-1 counterpart:** no large streaming GEMM has run on the ET-SoC-1. **Horace He's pair**
(item 3), still a question for him.

## Q137: prediction is the goal — a large GEMM predicted first, fp16's sign rest on the matched design, int8's B side on the rail, the memory path's encoded part settled, and the power-and-heat model M6 frozen and tested blind (Mon 2 Nov 2026, week 45; five card experiments on three cards, no WRITE command sent)

The owner's words, Mon 2 Nov 2026 about 15:20 PST: *"yes, run them. The overall goal is to be able to predict the power requirement
and heat generation of various kinds of numeric computations as accurately as possible."* (Q137; "them" are section 11's items 1, 5,
12, 10 and 6 of the [kinds page](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open) at `9fdb534bbc77`). Every card run was pre-registered and pushed before its claim and
sent reads only, under Q136's three rules (no power cycle, read-only PMIC voltage reads, every command classified before the first
launch). E138, E139 and E140 were re-derived by separate verifiers (holds with fixes); E136 and E137 by their own lanes' independent
code at the desk; Q137(m) has no verifier of its own, and E140's verifier regenerated its freeze byte for byte. The rows carry the
corrected figures. Short names, under `docs/reports/data/`: `E136` `2026-11-02-e136-large-gemm/`, `E137`
`2026-11-02-e137-fp16-sign-rest/`, `E138` `2026-11-02-e138-int8-b-rail/`, `E139` `2026-11-02-e139-memory-split-more-passes/`,
`E140` `2026-11-02-e140-blind4/`, `QM` `2026-11-02-q137m-model/` (Q137(m)); the pool is `tools/claims-v3/kinds/blind4/`. **How to
read ±:** a ± or bracket is a 95% interval over independent passes (over blocks in E138) unless the row says otherwise; E136 ran one
run a cell in one session, so its errors are rms over cells and its T intervals are over launches; Q137(m)'s ± on a coefficient is
one jackknife s.e. by kind. 🛑 **To carry from here, beside the earlier sections' items:** (23) **a nested leave-one-kind-out score
is not an out-of-sample bound:** quote M6's power error from E140 (0.41 W rms on new kinds, biased high, 19 of 25 inside its 95%), not
from its nested score; (24) **for power on new kinds M5 is the better predictor** (E140 C1); (25) **heat over a short run follows the
board watts** at about 0.118 °C per W on aifoundry3, to about 0.2 °C on new kinds; long holds are not scored out of sample. *Corrected after verification (Mon 2 Nov 2026, the integration's own verifier; no number changed): the heat row's "before" is the unfitted prior rule on the 22 experiments' rows, not a nested score; M5 against M6 is compared on one set of tiles; a key's path.*

**Not established by Q137.** **A large GEMM:** the vendor's own binary, a second session or card, and why `g2k` runs 11–13% faster
than any arm says (E136). **Signs:** whether the two designs give the same share (R4), another card or clock, and heat (E137). **Which
multiplier:** Booth on B against none as a registered verdict, and glitches (E138). **The memory path:** another card or other
images, the 1.0 pJ split, the 256-bit path's place, E99's refit (E139). **The model:** a power model whose stated intervals cover on
new kinds; M6 with E138's per-position B prices and E139's encoded term; the cases of its six over-predicted tiles; long-hold heat out
of sample (M6's step response and plateaus are quoted from E97, E111 and E133, not scored); any card but aifoundry3 for the blind pool
(Q137(m), E140). **Not in this round:** items 2, 3, 4, 7, 8, 9, 11 and 13–16, and every governor move.

## Q138: settle the power model's open questions — M7 frozen and scored blind on three cards, heat over minutes scored blind on two, int8's B side on a second card, C's stores refuted as a time term, the open single-bit positions priced, int8's excess with two A rows, and the model checked on two GPU types (Mon 2 Nov 2026, week 45; nine card sessions on three cards and two GPU runs on Modal, no WRITE command sent)

The owner's words, Mon 2 Nov 2026 about 18:40 PST: *"all cards are available, feel free to settle any unanswered questions
regarding horace's power model using [removed: the private archive's host], runs on aifoundry and modal for sanity checks on GPUs
(don't exceed $20 on modal)"* (Q138), with Q137's goal, to predict the power and heat of numeric work as accurately as possible.
The targets are the [kinds page](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open)'s section 11 as
at `724c17d3dc0b` and its "After Q137, the next steps in order". Every card run and GPU run was pre-registered and pushed before
its claim or first GPU call; every card run sent reads only, under Q136's three rules (no power cycle, read-only PMIC voltage
reads, every command classified before the first launch), and nothing was set; the GPU runs read NVML only. Every lane but Q138(x)
was re-derived by a separate verifier (all sixteen: holds with fixes) and corrected in its own commit; the rows carry the
corrected figures. Short names, under `docs/reports/data/2026-11-02-`: `E141` `e141-blind5-aifoundry3/`, `E142`
`e142-blind5-card1/`, `E143` `e143-blind5-aifoundry2/`, `E144` `e144-heat-minutes/`, `E144b` `e144-heat-minutes-aifoundry2/`,
`E145` `e145-int8-b-card1/`, `E146` `e146-gemm-stores/`, `E147` `e147-bit-prices-rest/`, `E148` `e148-int8-excess/`, `E149`
`e149-gpu-model-check/`, `E150` `e150-second-gpu/`, `QM` `q138m-model/` (Q138(m)), `QT` `q138t-heat-model/` (Q138(t)), `QG`
`q138g-int8-glitch/` (Q138(g)); the pool and its predictions are `tools/claims-v3/kinds/blind5/` (`B5`). **How to read ±:** a ± or
bracket is a 95% interval over independent passes (over blocks in E145 and E147, over tiles for E143's rms) unless the row says
otherwise; E144 and E144b ran one session each, so their errors are point readings with no interval; Q138(m)'s figures are nested
leave-one-kind-out, out of kind and not blind; a GPU row's ± is one standard error, and so are M7's coefficients (jackknife by kind) and E146's post hoc store energy (over 12 per-pass contrasts), as their rows say. 🛑 **To carry from here, beside the earlier
sections' items:** (26) **quote M7's power error on new kinds from the blind pools, on the footing of its training rows:** E141's
−0.23 W float bias and its fp16 FAIL follow the session's reduction footing, its own leakage fit and its rule for the meter's dips (post hoc on the training footing, +0.00 to −0.13 W),
and on card 1 M7 shows no gain over M6 (E142); (27) **M7's power bands are calibrated, not sharper:** its coverage gain over M6 is
band width (E141 C3, E142 C1); (28) **C's store cadence is not a term of a large GEMM's time** where C stays in the L3 (E146);
(29) **no per-kind heat coefficient** is supported on aifoundry2 (E144b C1); (30) **quote R5's die FAIL as E136's law's; what is open for a large GEMM on a new shape is its sustained power and governed clock:** E136's frozen two-parameter law, fed G2's power, read `F6`'s sustained die 3.6–4.6 °C low (R5 FAIL ×2), while its bursts held to 0.98 °C rms; given each run's measured board watts, Q138(t)'s frozen chain reads that die to −0.02 to +0.69 °C on the three anchors (about +1.0 / +1.1 °C on a whole-degree rest anchor averaged by sample), so within about 1 °C, and 56 sustained runs in four large-GEMM sessions to 0.47–0.63 °C rms, where E136's law on the same watts reads 2.18 °C (25 of the 56 inside the law's own fit; 2.25 °C on the 14 it never saw, where the chain reads 0.45–0.49 °C) (post hoc, [Q139(c)](../reports/data/2026-11-03-q139c-critic-checks/README.md)); fed G2's power instead of the trace, the chain reads `F6` 1.2–3.2 °C low, so the miss is G2's sustained power (3.65 / 2.76 W low) and clock (800 MHz where the governor held 745.1) (E146). *Corrected after verification (Tue 3 Nov 2026, the integration's own verifier; no measured number changed): item 7 was narrowed by pool 5's real tiles, not left, so the item table gains its row and the lists of items not in this round drop it, here and in 02; item 1's row, the large GEMM's prediction row and E146's claims gain the fresh shape `F6`'s registered sustained-die miss, +4.62 / +3.57 °C (R5 FAIL ×2), and its governed clock, 745.1 MHz where G2 said 800; item (30) is new; (26) names the whole footing, the leakage fit and the dip rule (E141 C1); M7's coefficients and E146's store energy are labelled ± 1 s.e.* *Corrected by Q139(c) (Tue 3 Nov 2026, post hoc, after Q138's final critic, whose three desk checks it re-derived with its own code in [`2026-11-03-q139c-critic-checks/`](../reports/data/2026-11-03-q139c-critic-checks/README.md); no measured, frozen or registered number changed): (30) said a large GEMM's sustained die on a new shape "is not yet predicted", but R5's die FAIL is E136's two-parameter law fed G2's power, and given the measured watts Q138(t)'s frozen chain reads that die to within 0.7 °C, so the open part is the sustained power and governed clock (here, item 1's row, the `F6` row and Not established); item 6's row said "no float offset measured on a matched footing", which E141's C1 contradicts at the dips-kept training footing (B1 −0.121 and −0.133 W FAIL HIGH at 0.2163 and 0.2217 W/°C); the card term is scored (E142's X1 FAILS for floats at the registered inputs, X2 passes for int8; post hoc); the governed clock's row gives M7's end-to-end 13.9 MHz and 2.35 W beside the in-sample reference.* *Corrected after verification (Tue 3 Nov 2026, about 05:20 PST, Q139(c)'s own verifier, which re-derived its three checks with its own code; no measured, frozen or registered number changed): `F6`'s chain error given the watts is −0.02 to +0.69 °C on the three anchors (int8 full −0.02 on its run's start; this said "+0.1 to +0.7" here and "+0.12 to +0.69 °C by anchor" in the `F6` row), and about +1.0 / +1.1 °C when the whole-degree rest die, 47 °C then 46 °C inside the anchor's window, is averaged by sample, so "within 0.7 °C" (in the note above, item 1's row and Not established) is "within about 1 °C"; E136's law's 2.18 °C over the 56 includes the 25 runs its sustained weight was fitted on (2.25 °C on the 14 it never saw); the card ratio's 0.931–1.015 (item 6's row and Not established) pairs one card's uncorrected leakage with the other's corrected, and over the 60 leakage-corrected pairs it runs 0.953–0.990; E142's claim row gains the post hoc X1/X2 score ([`2026-11-03-q139c-critic-checks/`](../reports/data/2026-11-03-q139c-critic-checks/README.md)).*

**Not established by Q138.** **The power model:** a blind test scored on one footing shared with its training rows; int8's three
steady misses (`b_line_zero_one`, `powerlaw_w` about 0.6 W high, a real w8a8 tile 0.5 W low); the card term at 1% (E142's X1/X2, scored post hoc by Q139(c): X1 FAILS for floats at the registered inputs,
X2 passes for int8, and the float ratio moves about 0.95–0.99 with the leakage-corrected reduction footings, 0.93–1.02 only with one card's leakage left uncorrected); an exponent term in M7's form (E147); aifoundry2's zeros level, a cap term rising with overshoot, a D scale by format and
a same-session clock anchor, found post hoc (E143); a check of C on file tiles. **Heat:** a hold steady by a registered test,
replicates, an inlet thermometer, a die reading finer than a whole degree, a matched-watts test of format, random fp32 at full
duty on aifoundry3 (E144, E144b). **Which multiplier:** the structural verdict with a registered spike filter, or on aifoundry3;
why the even B bits cost more (Q138(g) excludes glitches in these netlists). **A large GEMM:** the vendor's own binary, a second
card, an L3 retention curve by reuse distance, the KB = 1 shapes' A lines, a sustained power and governed clock on a new shape (E146 R5; given the measured watts the frozen chain reads the sustained die to within about 1 °C, post hoc, Q139(c)). **int8's excess:** the per-op premise, which
count the per-row part follows, what stays at one row (E148). **GPUs:** which pairing an A100 pays for, a constant's own cost, a
lower cap, his board, an H100, a later generation under its cap (E149, E150). **Not in this round:** items 3, 4, 5, 10, 13, 14
and 16; every governor move; E117 stays not run.

## Q139: Q138's final critic's open list — one reduction footing, M8 frozen and scored blind on pool 6 on three cards with every launch's C checked, heat over minutes predicted from the bits, a large GEMM's sustained power and clock blind, the L3's retention, the clock/voltage term, the gate's dose, int8's structure and its floor by shape, the memory path on a second card, and a GPU's operand pairing (Tue 3 Nov 2026, week 45; eleven card sessions on three cards and one GPU run on Modal, no WRITE command sent)

The owner's mandate is his words of Mon 2 Nov 2026 about 18:40 PST, carried into Tue 3 Nov: *"all cards are available, feel free
to settle any unanswered questions regarding horace's power model using [removed: the private archive's host], runs on aifoundry
and modal for sanity checks on GPUs (don't exceed $20 on modal)"*, with Q137's goal, to predict the power and heat of numeric work
as accurately as possible; and, after the session had stopped and been revived, at about 15:47 PST: *"approved both aifoundry and
[removed: the private archive's host], continue"* (Q139). The targets are Q138's final critic's open list (ranks 1–16) and its
faults, against the [kinds page](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open)'s section 11 as
at `8ec9ead107aa`. Every card run and the GPU run was pre-registered and pushed before its claim or first GPU call; every card run
sent reads only, under Q136's three rules, and nothing was set; the GPU run read NVML only. Every lane but Q139(x) was re-derived
by a separate verifier (all twenty-one: holds with fixes) and corrected in its own commit; the rows carry the corrected figures.
**The records disclose two things** ([02-requests.md](02-requests.md), Q139): the session running the round stopped at about 07:46
PST on a usage limit, and twelve of its agents with it, so E160's finished queue was collected from the box only at 15:56 PST,
E152's and E156's verifiers ran again in the evening, and E153, E154, E157 and E161 ran in the evening; and the day's first card
post, left holding E160's claim when the session stopped, was later deleted by someone else. Short names, under
`docs/reports/data/2026-11-03-`: `E151` `e151-int8-b-aifoundry3/`, `E152` `e152-gate-dose/`, `E153` `e153-blind6-aifoundry3/`,
`E154` `e154-heat-e2e-aifoundry3/`, `E155` `e155-int8-floor-rows/`, `E156` `e156-memory-images-card1/`, `E157`
`e157-blind6-card1/`, `E158` `e158-l3-retention/`, `E159` `e159-gemm-sustained/`, `E160` `e160-blind6-aifoundry2/`, `E161`
`e161-heat-e2e-aifoundry2/`, `E162` `e162-gpu-row-k/`, `QC` `q139c-critic-checks/` (Q139(c)), `QU` `q139u-footing/` (Q139(u)),
`QS` `q139s-clock-voltage/` (Q139(s)), `QQ` `q139q-gemm-g3/` (Q139(q)), `QM8` `q139m-model/` (Q139(m)), `QE`
`q139e-heat-end-to-end/` (Q139(e)); the pool and its predictions are `tools/claims-v3/kinds/blind6/` (`B6`), the check of C
`tools/claims-v3/ccheck/` (`CC`). **How to read ±:** a ± or bracket is a 95% interval over independent passes unless the row says
otherwise: over tiles for a blind rms and for the M8 − M7 and M8 − M6 contrasts (a bootstrap inside one session), over holds for
E159's (post hoc); E154 and E161 ran one session each, so their errors are point readings with no interval; Q139(m)'s and
Q139(q)'s figures are nested and out of kind, not blind; Q139(u)'s, Q139(s)'s and Q139(c)'s are post hoc; a GPU row's ± is one
standard error. 🛑 **To carry from here, beside the earlier sections' items:** (31) **for a new kind's power quote M7 or M6, and
M8's blind error on the one footing:** M8 is resolved worse than M7 on aifoundry3 (M2) and not better than M6 on either 600 MHz
card (E153, E157), though its float bias is gone on the footing; (32) **reduce every 600 MHz row on Q139(u)'s footing** (the
frozen per-card slopes and A2 once), and keep aifoundry2's dips, its governor's own phases (Q139(u) C1); (33) **E160 read pool 6's
new ranges first** (int8's 32-bit wrap, full-range int8, wide fp16 exponents, real tiles); E153's and E157's are the first checked
reads on their cards (E157 C2); (34) **rank 4's two halves have first blind scores, not answers** (E154 C1, E161 C3), and E97's
0.3–0.8 °C is a move within its one session, not between sessions (E144 C3); (35) **G3 FAILS every registered per-hold rule:**
quote it beside its rms gain over E136's law (E159); (36) **E153's amendment A1 followed an interim look, and no verdict moved**
(E153 C1). *Corrected after the integration's own verifier (Tue 3 Nov 2026, about 23:10 PST; no measured number changed): the
Validity row's before names the kinds loop, whose host checks C only on its own small-integer tiles, while the
large-GEMM power runs (E136, E146, and E158 and E159 in Q139) checked C in every run against their harness's host
reference (R0 PASS); and E158's 6.0–6.3% on new shapes is quoted beside its registered R4 NOT BETTER (1.2 of the 2
points it needed; 10.9% → 10.6% on all 24 cells) in the item-1 row, "So:" and the superseded list, as its prediction
row and claim row already did.* *Corrected by Q140(c) (Wed 4 Nov 2026, post hoc, after Q139's final critic, whose four desk checks it re-derived with its own code in [`2026-11-04-q140c-critic-checks/`](../reports/data/2026-11-04-q140c-critic-checks/README.md); no measured, frozen or registered number changed), each by a dated clause in the row it concerns, the row's own words kept: the board's exponent does not carry out of sample (on E160, run after it was committed, n = 2.28 reads the governed clock 10.50 MHz rms against M7's 4.89); heat over minutes from the bits is not like for like between the cards (aifoundry3's chain ran on the session's realized dispatch records, aifoundry2's assumed continuous work), and E161's two holds are not averaged; aifoundry2's burst errors carry a session offset in the clock and a bias both sessions share in the board; the shares of a kind's watts and the intervals are given; card 1's S1 and S2 do not test an unmeasured card; and the 4.5 s heat figure is a skill on the floats.*

**So:** on the one footing, a new kind's power at 600 MHz is predicted blind to 0.10–0.39 W rms by card and format, about 1–2% of
a float kind's switching watts and 4–10% of an int8 kind's, unbiased on the floats on both cards; M8 is no better than M7 or M6,
and its new int8 term over-corrects where it acts most. Heat over a 4.5 s run holds at about 0.1–0.2 °C, and over ten minutes,
predicted from the bits alone, to about ±0.4 °C on aifoundry3 and about 0.4–0.9 °C on governed aifoundry2, from one session each.
A governed large GEMM's sustained clock, board and die come within 8.7 MHz, 1.9 W and 1.0 °C blind, though no per-hold rule
passes, and its time per op on new shapes to about 6% (R4 NOT BETTER by its 2-point bar). A card nobody has measured still needs a measured reference.

*Corrected by Q140(c) (Wed 4 Nov 2026, post hoc; the paragraph above is left as written): the shares are 2.4–2.9% of an fp32 kind's switching watts, under 1% of fp16's and 6–7% of int8's (M8; 0.29–0.32 W over the 30 kinds, 95% over tiles 0.20–0.45 W); the 4.5 s heat figure is a skill on the floats, int8's rises being whole-degree readings; and the two cards' heat over minutes is not like for like: aifoundry3's 0.37 °C rms (95% on the sigma 0.22–1.06 °C) ran the chain on the session's realized dispatch records, while aifoundry2's plateau the bits set read −0.90 °C assuming continuous work (−0.39 °C at the kernel duty), and its −0.40 °C at the trip tests the trip rule, not the bits.*

*After Q140(c)'s verifier (Wed 4 Nov 2026, post hoc; the note above is left as written): each share is that format's own rms over its mean kind (M8 on aifoundry3 / card 1: fp32 0.330 / 0.386 W of 13.78 / 13.24 W, fp16 0.125 / 0.098 W of 17.89 / 17.26 W, int8 0.296 / 0.327 W of 4.92 / 4.76 W); the 30 kinds' 0.29–0.32 W is 2.1–2.4% of a mean fp32 kind ([`check-fix-figures.txt`](../reports/data/2026-11-04-q140c-critic-checks/check-fix-figures.txt), check 2).*

### What Q139 refuted, superseded or withdrew

| Was | Now | Where |
|---|---|---|
| (Q138's final critic, rank 1) the blind float bias set by each session's own leakage fit | one footing, frozen first; on it the float bias is inside B1 on both 600 MHz cards, post hoc on pool 5 and blind on pool 6; aifoundry2's dips-kept reduction is its footing | Q139(u), E153, E157 |
| (Q139(u), first) re-reduce E80 and E83 with A2 on aifoundry2 | withdrawn: there A2's dips are the governor's own phases | Q139(u) C1 |
| (rank 2's rule) M8 − M6 resolved below zero on both 600 MHz cards | not met on either; M8 resolved worse than M7 on aifoundry3 | E153, E157 |
| (Q139(m)) M8's int8 near-whole-byte discount, kept | contradicted post hoc on the tiles where it acts most, on both 600 MHz cards and in a 10-minute hold; no registered rule falsifies it | E153 C6, E157, E154 |
| (Q139(k), first) int8's C never read back on silicon | read on 90 small-integer launches by the host's own check on 25 September 2026; what had not been read was int8's wrap and full range | Q139(k) C1 |
| (E153's and E157's first text) the first silicon reads of pool 6's ranges | the first checked reads on their cards; E160 read them first, on aifoundry2 | E157 C2 (a dated note on E153's README, these records) |
| (Q139(s), first) the board's exponent 2.45 ± 0.03 | about 2.3 (2.26–2.31 at aifoundry2's measured 600 MHz slope) | Q139(s) C1 |
| (Q139(q), first) P0's level as the next term; k = 0.1 W/°C as a leakage | withdrawn: the next gain needs P0's per-shape level and the held-state clock together, and k is an effective constant | Q139(q) C1 |
| (E159, as registered) H1's clock miss is P0's level | post hoc, the governor's 600–700 MHz dips; about 3 W of its board deficit is its cold start | E159 C1, C2 |
| (E158) `F6`'s registered pass as the retention curve's test | a pooled pass one rung carries; quote the new shapes' 6.0–6.3% beside R4 NOT BETTER (1.2 of its 2 points) | E158 C1 |
| (E160's first text) pinned 7.45 → 3.89 MHz as a gain | that before is in sample; against E143 the pinned tiles are no better | E160 C2 |
| (E161, first) a work share of 0.94: G1Q +0.12 °C, G2Q −2.1 MHz | the kernel duty, 0.970: −0.39 °C and +5.6 MHz; M8's level the larger part | E161 C1 |
| (E154, first) rank 4's aifoundry3 half met on 2 of 4 holds; the heat path's miss an fp32 term | 1 of 4; the miss tracks the size of the rise, int8 included | E154 C1, C2 |
| (E152, first) linear within 3% of full scale | point departures; supported to about ±10% | E152 C1 |
| (E151, first) the per-position B price a property of the multiplier, not of these tiles | consistent with one, measured at one B base word | E151 C2 |
| (E156, first) the 128-bit addition excluded; the frozen +0.185 W per image met | excluded on card 1's primary only; the 0.05 W line put on the frozen term per image | E156 C3, C4 |
| (E162, first) K-steps of 8 | the row-lag is identified, the K-lag is not | E162 C1 |
| (Q139(e)'s README) E97's between-session plateau moves of 0.3–0.8 °C | a move within E97's one session (E144 C3; a dated note on Q139(e)'s README, these records) | E154 C13 |

**Not established by Q139.** **The power model:** M8 better than M7 or M6 anywhere; an exponent-field term, and a base-word term
for single bits (`fp32.b_onebit_b20_third`); int8 held-B and a bounded whole-byte discount; aifoundry2's governed board level, a
zeros level and a session term (the die at the start, the room); repeat D between days below 0.1–0.2 W on card 1; the card term
off the registered footing; an unmeasured card's scale from cheap reads (rank 5 stays open). **Heat:** a frozen duty term, M8's
level inside a hold, the chain's gain on large rises, a second session per card, an inlet thermometer, a die reading finer than a
whole degree. **A large GEMM:** a reference die and the governor's in-process clock for P0, a zeros level, a block-stride term,
the KB = 1 A lines, the vendor's binary, a second card. **The gate:** what spends its price, per-boundary and lane-assignment
costs, the A side's gate, int8's, other clocks and cards. **int8:** why the even B bits cost about twice Booth's count;
per-position B prices at other base words; what stays at one A row; the per-op premise below ±30 pJ. **The memory path:** the 1.0
pJ split, the 256-bit path, a second card's `E192`. **GPUs:** the K-lag, zeros along rows and along K, a later generation under
its cap, a lower cap. **Not in this round:** ranks 6 and 17 (the inlet thermometer; items 3, 4, 14 and 16, item 2's lower cap)
wait on the owner's word; every governor move; E117 stays not run.

## Q140, the desk half: Q139's final critic's open list frozen for the cards — its four desk checks and faults, aifoundry2's session terms, M9, blind pool 7 with every model's predictions and rank 4's rule, the heat replicates, G4 and a stride term (Wed 4 Nov 2026, week 45, overnight; desk only: no card, no device command, no Modal, no archive)

The owner's mandate is still his words of Mon 2 Nov 2026 about 18:40 PST (Q138), with Q137's goal, to predict the power and heat
of numeric work as accurately as possible; his last words are Q139's, of Tue 3 Nov about 15:47 PST, *"approved both aifoundry and
[removed: the private archive's host], continue"*, and he has not spoken since (Q140). The targets are Q139's final critic's open
list (ranks 1–17) and its faults (0–11), against the [kinds page](https://spacesheep.dev/@yaroslavvb/horace-kinds-of-numbers#11-what-is-still-open)'s
section 11 as at `d571349e663f`. This night ran the desk half only, to freeze what the card half needs *(dated, Wed 4 Nov 2026, after Q140's final critic, Q141(r): it froze the predictions and rules; the registrations, scorers and kits are Q141's, the section after this one)* (E163–E169, which wait on
his word on timing and on fresh lab approvals): no lane claimed a card, sent a device command, opened an ssh to a lab box,
read or wrote Discord, started a Modal app or read the archive, so **no row below is a new measurement or a blind score**. Each lane
that fits or predicts registered its candidate terms, folds and rules and pushed them before any fit, score or prediction; Q140(c)'s
checks are post hoc by design; M9's two parts were frozen before pool 7 entered the tree, and pool 7 was committed with every
model's predictions before any pool-7 claim. Every lane was re-derived by a separate verifier (all seven: holds with fixes) and
corrected in its own commits; the rows carry the corrected figures, and no verifier has re-read the fix passes. Short names, under
`docs/reports/data/2026-11-04-`: `Q140c/` `q140c-critic-checks/`, `Q140l/` `q140l-a2-session-terms/`, `Q140m/` `q140m-m9/`,
`Q140e/` `q140e-heat-replicates/`, `Q140q/` `q140q-gemm-g4-stride/`, `Q140x/` `q140x-blind7-notes/`; pool 7 and its predictions
are `tools/claims-v3/kinds/blind7/` (`B7`). **How to read ±:** a ± or bracket is a 95% interval unless the row says otherwise:
over sessions or runs for Q140(l)'s and Q140(q)'s held-out figures, which are leave one session out with the terms chosen on the
same folds unless the row says nested; Q140(m)'s are nested leave one kind out and out of kind, not blind; a bench recount's ± is
one standard error; Q140(c)'s rows and every row marked post hoc were read after the data. *(Dated, Wed 4 Nov 2026, after
Q140(r)'s verifier: the term table's fitted prices, M8's −56.8 ± 29.4 and M9's −29.3 ± 9.4 fJ a bit, `bpp_r4`'s +2.205 ± 0.271 and
`xstage`'s +0.65 ± 0.36 fJ, are ± one standard error, the jackknife by kind (Q140(m)'s README, section 3), and so is the
paired-anchor card scale, −0.15% (±0.10%) for floats and −1.0% ± 1.5% for int8, from the anchors' passes (Q140(c) check 3); a 95%
interval is about twice as wide, so int8's scale is about ±3% at 95%.)* *(Dated again, Wed 4 Nov 2026, after the records' second
verifier: that doubling holds for the jackknife prices, which rest on 66–370 kinds a family, but not for the anchor scale. Its
standard error rests on a few passes: card 1's three passes of E78's int8 anchor carry almost all of int8's (2.14 degrees of
freedom), and pool 6's anchor errors are assumed equal to E78's, not measured. With t quantiles, as the lab's pass intervals take
them, int8's scale is ±4.1–6.1% at 95%, not about ±3%, and the floats' ±0.23–0.26%
([`2026-11-04-q140-anchor-scale-t95/`](../reports/data/2026-11-04-q140-anchor-scale-t95/README.md)).)* *(Dated a third time, Wed 4 Nov 2026, after Q140's final critic, Q141(r): pool 6's random anchors have committed passes of their own, one run a pass, in E153's and E157's runs; with each anchor's own passes, on both cards, int8's scale is ±3.7% at 95% at 3.06 Welch degrees of freedom and the floats' ±0.24% at 7.36, so ±4.1–6.1% is too wide; the points, −1.04% and −0.15%, do not move ([the README's dated note](../reports/data/2026-11-04-q140-anchor-scale-t95/README.md.dated-note.md)).)* Pool 7 is described by counts and
classes only, until its runs. 🛑 **To carry from here, beside the earlier sections' items:** (37) **no Q140 figure is a blind
score:** M9, G4, the heat replicates and rank 4's rule are frozen, and E163–E169 score them; (38) **quote Q140(l)'s and Q140(q)'s

**So:** after the desk half every term the critic named has a registered test frozen for the cards, and none has a blind score yet.
At the desk, out of kind or held out: int8's power gains a term that works (0.25–0.26 → 0.18 W), the floats none; aifoundry2's
bursts lose about half of their board error and of their clock's session offset to three session terms, with the clock still the
open part (5.0–6.9 MHz); a governed large GEMM's held-out board improves (1.89 → 1.3–1.5 W) and its clock does not; the board's
exponent, a start-die term for held windows and a stride term from the L3's public set mapping fail; and part of the one tile every
model missed by about 1 W is the bench's own counting. Heat gets one duty convention and a long hold, frozen, with no new score.

**Not established by Q140's desk half.** Any blind score: M9, G4, the replicates and rank 4's rule wait on E163–E169. **The power
model:** M9 better than M6 on new kinds; a float term that lowers new kinds' error (the exponent-heavy excess, +0.18 W on pool 6, is
unexplained); the bit-20 miss split between the bench's averaging and a price (at 27 points 10 of 16 calibration tiles still miss
the bound); aifoundry2's governed clock below the critic's 4 MHz (5.0 MHz held out, session means about ±3 MHz); rank 8's desk rule
(3 of 4); one die law for heat over minutes and the large GEMM; the 82 W admission with M9's lower levels. **Heat:** a slow stage or
a gain error on large rises (E166); a second session per card; an inlet thermometer; a die reading finer than a whole degree. **A
large GEMM:** G4's clock (no gain); a stride term (E169); a DRAM channel or bank term (no public source for the interleave); the
vendor's binary; a second card. **A card nobody has measured:** rank 4's rule on one card pair only. **Not in this round:** rank 7
(card 1's day-to-day floor), ranks 10–16, and rank 17 (items 3, 4, 14 and 16, item 2's lower cap, the inlet thermometer, aifoundry2's
PMIC over-power configuration), which waits on the owner's word; every governor move; E117 stays not run.

### The readiness verdict and the plan (Q141(f), about 12:20–13:10 PST)

**All seven runs ready to claim**, with only the box steps left after the owner's go and fresh lab approvals. Three cards in
parallel, serial on each, about 312–340 card-minutes (caps 384): on aifoundry3 E166 then E163, released about T+158 min; on
aifoundry1's card 1 E164, released about T+80; on aifoundry2 four box dry passes, E165, the latch check, E167, E168 and E169,
released about T+210–230. **The last release comes about 3.5–4 h after the go, not 3–3.5 h**: E167 holds aifoundry2 about 66–75
minutes of wall time, and the four dry passes there must come before E165 (F5). The full plan needs the last end at least about 4
h 15 min after the go and the last start at least 3 h 30 min after it. Subsets: (a) about 1.5 h, E163 (FULL or TWO) with E164 and
E165, which gives rank 1 on both 600 MHz cards, rank 4's pair and rank 2; (b) about 2.75 h, everything but E168 and E169; (c)
about 3.5–4 h, all seven. E166 has no subset.

## Ridge points (derived; no card time)

Peak compute divided by each level's measured bandwidth, at 600 MHz on 1,024 minions. Kind `D`: arithmetic on
measured numbers, not a new measurement. The bandwidths are the 2026-09-18 reports' (reproduced within 0.3% by the
2026-09-23 reruns on both cards, the rl-pass runs the energy manual pools); the energy balance points use the energy manual's data
(`docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/manual.json`). Recompute every row with `python3 scripts/ridge-points.py`.
Values are FLOP (int8: OP) per byte fetched, fp32 / fp16 / int8.

| Claim | Value | Kind | Source | Verify at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tensor peak per minion-cycle | **16 / 32 / 128** ops (9.83 TFLOP/s, 19.7 TFLOP/s, 78.6 TOP/s on 1,024 minions at 600 MHz) | X | Minion VPU Specification §2; matmul report | `scripts/ridge-points.py`, `PEAK` |
| Ridge, own shire (L2 or L2 scratchpad) | **4 / 8 / 32** at 4.0 B per minion-cycle (2.46 TB/s); 2 / 4 / 16 at the banks' 256 B per shire-cycle | D | memory-hierarchy streaming probes | `docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-memhier-aifoundry2/energy*/runs.jsonl`, configs `l2`, `scp-local` |
| Ridge, L3 or another shire's scratchpad | **10 / 20 / 80** at 0.96–0.98 TB/s | D | same, configs `l3`, `scp-remote`, 600 MHz launches only | same file, `implied_ghz` 0.59–0.61 |
| Ridge, DRAM | **130 / 259 / 1,036** at 76 GB/s | D | same, config `dram`; 76 GB/s on both cards in the 23 September reruns (the sparsity report's shorter probe read 72 on aifoundry3) | same file; `docs/reports/data/2026-09-18-sparsity-aifoundry3/tload-dram-all.jsonl` |
| Ridge, host over PCIe Gen4 x8 | **624 / 1,248 / 4,993** at 15.75 GB/s, the link figure; at the host-to-device DMA rate measured on 27 September (12.46–12.60 GB/s, E50), **780–789 / 1,560–1,578 / 6,242–6,314** | X; D on E50 | the link figure: 16 GT/s × 8 lanes × 128/130 ÷ 8 (datasheet §1: Gen4 x8); the measured rate: E50 (the ridge page still quotes only the link figure) | `docs/reports/data/2026-09-27-pcie/pcie.json`, `bw.<card>.h2d.dma[bytes=268435456].gbs.mean`; the chip diagram's fact `ridge.levels` (`docs/reports/data/2026-09-27-chip-diagram/build_facts.py`) |
| Intensity of one full-size TensorFMA | **4 / 8 / 16** per byte (2 KB of A and B per op) | D | PRM ch. 9 | `scripts/ridge-points.py`, `OP_BYTES` |
| Smallest DRAM-resident C block that is compute-bound | about **520 × 520** (fp32, fp16), **1,040 × 1,040** (int8) | D | H/e ≥ ridge, H the harmonic mean of the block's sides, e = bytes per element | the report's "What it takes to reach them" |
| Energy balance, fp32: FLOP per byte at which moving a byte costs as much energy as the arithmetic | L1 0.15 (`flw.ps` against `fmadd.ps`, random data), own shire 0.87, other shire 2.3, L3 3.6, DRAM **42** (random fp32 at 2.89 pJ per FLOP over idle; on all-zero operands, 0.21 pJ, mostly the awake-core floor, every level from the shire outward lies above its time ridge) | D | energy manual §3.2 (tensor unit), §4.1 (the L1 row), §4.2 (levels) | `docs/reports/data/2026-09-23-energy-manual/manual.json` (`tensor.bars`, reruns; `catalogue.combined`: `flw.ps/random/h2`, `fmadd.ps/random/h2`, `tload/*`); the page's `ridge-data` `energy` |

Two corrections to earlier reports came out of this, both verified in the raw data:

- The memory-hierarchy report's first version averaged launches taken at 600–800 MHz (2.80 TB/s for L2, "about 144
  B/cycle"). At 600 MHz the cycle counters give **2.45 TB/s** and **128 B per shire-cycle**, which that report now
  prints.
- The **128,000 MB/s** DRAM figure the runtime reports is a placeholder constant in the service-processor firmware
  (`device-bootloaders/src/ServiceProcessorBL2/include/mem_controller.h`), not a measurement or a configured rate.

| A100 matmul measured by Horace He | 257 TFLOPS random / 295 TFLOPS zeros, 16-bit by the A100's peaks, if his TFLOPS count as his script's do (the post names no format; this row said bf16 until 1 Nov 2026), 330 W limit, 88 W idle (dated note, 2 Nov 2026: his own loop on two A100-SXM4-40GB boards at 400 W reads zeros 293.25 / 293.06 and random normal 271.37 / 269.61 TFLOPS, and the boards idle at 55.9–58.9 W; E135) | X | R8 |
| A100 energy per FLOP | 1.28 pJ per FLOP at 16 bits, as above (330 W / 257 TFLOPS); against this card 5.4× better than its fp32 (6.96 pJ) and 2.6× than its fp16 (3.3 pJ); the A100's fp32 CUDA-core datasheet figure (19.5 TFLOPS at 400 W) is 20.5 pJ (dated note, 2 Nov 2026: measured at 400 W, board power over each kind's own rate, sustained, bf16 random normal 1.49–1.51 pJ per FLOP and zeros 0.86–0.89, at each kind's own clock in an 8192³ GEMM; not like for like with this card's resident-loop prices; E135) | D | R7, R8, E15 |
| A100 clock under Horace He's 330 W cap | 1,160–1,410 MHz, probably about 1,230 (257 of 312 peak TFLOPS needs at least 1,160) (dated note, 2 Nov 2026: post hoc, E135's relation puts random bf16 at 330 W near 1,160 MHz on its unit B, 1,410 ÷ its ×1.217, a model fitted after the data on a board that is not his; at 400 W random bf16 measured 1,267–1,277 MHz) | D, A | R7, R8 |
| **A100 core voltage** | **0.85 V** | **A** | **Assumption.** Not published. 0.75 V is nominal for 7 nm per R6; GPUs run above nominal at full clock. The range 0.75–0.95 V changes the V² ratio from 2.1× to 3.3× |
| ET-SoC-1: transistors, die, mask layers | over 24 B, 570 mm², 89 layers | X | R6 |
| ET-SoC-1: memory | 256-bit LPDDR4x, 137 GB/s per chip | X | R6 |
| Esperanto's modelled chip power vs voltage | 275 W highest, 164 W @0.75 V, 118 W @0.67 V, ~20 W @0.4 V, 8.5 W @0.3 V | X | R6 — **modelled, with cores re-synthesised per voltage point**, not measured |
| Esperanto's per-core design target | 10 mW at 1 GHz, 0.425 V, 0.04 nF | X | R6 |

## Numbers that are *not* established

- **Anything about this chip at 0.4 V.** This card's firmware offers 600, 700 and 800 MHz at 0.52–0.62 V and
  nothing lower. Esperanto's 20 W headline is at an operating point never exercised here.
- **Per-FLOP efficiency on the workload the chip was designed for** (int8 with sparse memory access) against a
  GPU. The int8 arithmetic is 19× cheaper than fp32 here (E15), but no GPU comparison was run for it.
- **Anything requiring modified firmware:** per-event PMU counters on silicon, SRAM ECC error counts, the
  UltraSoC debug fabric. See Q7 in [02-requests.md](02-requests.md).
- **The thermal network in any other chassis.** 1.47 °C/W is this card in this desktop box, which idles at
  62–80 °C, and the flip budget built on it is also that day's room: the model's ambient is 22.8 °C, and each degree
  warmer takes 0.68 W (1/1.47) off it (Horace §8).
- **Whether the taped-out ET-SoC-1 ever drives its sleep transistors.** The chip-level netlist is not in the
  open drop; the tie-off is a fact about the Erbium configuration, and the card only shows that nothing
  observable uses them.
- ~~**Why aifoundry3's flashed TDP is 0 W.**~~ Answered on 2026-09-25: it is not flashed; a boot service on
  aifoundry3 sets it at every boot (14-card-behaviour.md).
- **Whether the 8% switching-power gap between the two cards is silicon, package or board regulator.**
  Separating those needs a third working card: aifoundry1's card 1 works since 25 September, and the version-3
  campaign measured it. It did not settle the question: E40's hot-against-cool test reads "not separated" on every
  card, and E45 puts the catalogue's temperature coefficient at +0.48 [0.07–0.90] %/°C on aifoundry2 and +0.30
  [0.18–0.42] on aifoundry3, which the registered rule reads as temperature, not the card.
- ~~**Per-flip energies outside the tensor unit and the mesh links**~~ **Partly established on 2026-10-20 and 10-21
  (E60, E61):** the vector integer unit's one-gate prices are measured — a read-port bit into an XOR 20.83 ± 0.10 fJ,
  a result bit 29.67 ± 0.15, an input plus an output bit 50.7 ± 0.6, a carry 0.98 ± 0.80, a register-file cell
  0.76 ± 0.32 (under-resolved), and the gate's own kind at most 2.7 fJ of about 53. Still not established: any
  per-flip price in the load/store path, the register file's own cells to better than ±40%, the clock tree's share,
  and **the split of the unsensed 15 W of idle**. E30 attributes the unmetered part of what a workload adds above
  idle (delivery losses plus a DRAM term, fitted over configuration means) and meters DRAM by droop; the idle
  12–16 W (12–13 W on aifoundry3 at 51–56 °C, 14–16 W on aifoundry2 at 66–82 °C) and the DRAM term's split below its
  regulator stay inferred.
- **How many transistor-level toggles one flipped operand bit causes** (E60, 2026-10-20). Dividing the measured
  116.7 / 129.9 fJ by the RTL's counted net toggles gives 0.301 and 0.119 fJ a toggle — 2.5× apart for what should be
  one number, so either the count or the cascade model is wrong. Until the denominator is settled, no "fJ per net
  toggle" from this work is quotable, and neither is "about 1,050 toggles per flipped bit", which is a stream average
  over a whole kernel and not one flip's cascade.
- **What one transistor switching costs on this chip, at its own 0.517 V.** Never measured on this silicon. The
  closest stand-in is ASAP7, a predictive 7 nm kit characterised at 0.7 V, where a minimum inverter's input pin
  presents **0.620 fF** (Q126(d), `2026-10-26-asap7-cell-energies/asap7-cell-energies.json`,
  `cells.INVx1.inputs.A.cap_fF`, read from `asap7sc7p5t_INVBUF_RVT_TT_nldm_220122.lib`, RVT TT). Charging that
  through this chip's own 0.517 V is ½CV² = **83 aJ**, about **2,000 electrons**, some **26,000×** Landauer's
  kT ln 2 = 3.17 zJ at 58 °C; at the kit's own 0.7 V it is 152 aJ and 2,710 electrons. Driving the gate is not all
  of it: the same cell's internal energy is 0.049 fJ a transition at 0.7 V, and Q126(d) part 2 measures 0.0875 fJ
  per cycle in SPICE on the kit's own devices. All of that is a different process at a different voltage, not a
  measurement of this chip. (**Corrected 26 Oct 2026**: first written as "83 aJ there … about 24,500×", which
  attributed the 0.517 V figures to 0.7 V and divided by Landauer at 80 °C under a 58 °C label.)
- **The flip model has no argument for clock, supply voltage, die temperature or card** (E9, E17; measured against
  E64). Its static term is one number, 27.1 W, where the card's data-independent floor is 25.46 / 28.24 / 33.61 W at
  600 / 700 / 800 MHz: **−19% at 800 MHz**. Nothing in the model knows about the governor either. Its held-out error
  is 0.906–1.008 W rms (E38, FAIL against a registered 0.6 W), and it has never been scored on fp16, int8, memory,
  mesh or gather loads at all.
- **Millimetres, anywhere in the camera work** (E62, E63). The registered 10% gate for a scale bar was not met (two
  routes to it disagree by 64%), so every camera finding is a direction and an amplitude, never a distance on the die.
- **A steady camera gain** (E63). The transfer drifted 59.0 → 52.9 → 47.8 → 42.4 mK/W over one hour as the board and
  the camera warmed, the lag 87° → 109°, so a thermal camera used as a wattmeter needs its own reference rung near
  every reading. The post-hoc second point at 0.15 Hz (18.4 ± 5.4 mK/W, a ratio of 0.34 ± 0.10) is two points of a
  transfer function, not a calibrated instrument: it rests on four camera third harmonics none of which clears 2 SE
  on its own, and the single pole fitted to it is bounded below and not above.
- **Phonons, and anything between the transistor and the heat the camera sees.** The word does not appear in this
  repository; the chain from a switching device to a millikelvin on the back of the board is not written down.
- **Whether the heat-per-mm "ones" cost is resting-at-zero logic or precharged structures**, and how a hop's
  energy splits between router and wire (E31, E32). The mesh rail's meter gain has not been checked independently,
  and whether the links are low-swing, which the V² scaling assumes they are not, is unknown.
- **Whether a refresh or another stream's access closed each hart's row between its visits, or the activation is
  merely small**; E28 cannot tell. (The controller runs an open-page policy: rows close at a refresh or at another
  row's access, not on a timer; E1.)
- **Any *catalogue* table at 700 or 800 MHz.** The V²f ratios say what to expect, and since 20 October some
  individual numbers are measured there — E60's ladders and one-gate prices on the two governor-free cards at 800 MHz
  and 0.604–0.618 V, E63's 154 fJ per flipped bit, E64's floor at 600 / 700 / 800 MHz — but the energy catalogue, the
  memory levels and the mesh rows were all taken at 600 MHz and have not been re-measured at another clock.
- **A relay whose working set exceeds the 80 MB of scratchpad.** That is where on-chip hand-off would be the
  only option rather than the faster one; the flow control for it was not built.
- **Why Ivan's 6% differs from both of our numbers.** His code was not run.
- **Whether a hot line makes its shire measurably hotter.** The thermal telemetry is one chip-wide mean.
- **aifoundry3's thermal network.** Its heatsink is visibly faster than aifoundry2's, and no heat-then-cool
  characterisation was run on it. Do not apply aifoundry2's 1.47 °C/W to it.
- ~~**Whether the governor's power branch behaves as written.**~~ **Established on 2026-09-22 (E21):** on
  aifoundry3 it logs a throttle-down at every kernel start and can never step up, on a die 15 °C below the thermal
  threshold.
- **Why aifoundry3's board and rail readings change only about every 250 ms**, against 133 ms on aifoundry2: its
  service-processor loop or its PMIC. E41 narrows it to the loop's length, not why: with no poller aifoundry3's
  service-processor pass takes 224.1–224.5 ms, against 133.2 ms on aifoundry2 and 134.8 ms on aifoundry1's card 1.
- **Exactly which firmware build the cards run.** Their trace strings match service-processor firmware older than
  et-platform commit `60b40c10f` (24 September 2024); release 1.3.1's BL2 0.20.0 has `ffca4cbb4` as its closest
  public source, and E51's development data fit that build's governor, but the build itself is not in the tree, and
  E51's frozen validation left that theory (TH2) untested: 2 of its 46 idle loop intervals fell 17–21 ms off the grid,
  outside the 15 ms tolerance, and it saw 16 of the 20 descents its rule needs.
- **Whether the governor acts on the mean or on the hottest sensor, by a registered test** (E51). The frozen validation
  left TH1 untested: 7 of 9 separating runs fitted the mean, where G1-T asks for 80%, and 4 idle cycles separated the two,
  where I1 asks for 5; no run or cycle fitted the hottest sensor, and G1-H passed (17 holds at 800 MHz with the hottest
  sensor at 67 °C or more). The answer rests on the firmware source.
- **Whether placement delays the first throttle on aifoundry2** (E51, Q2). In each of the validation's 4 complete blocks
  the perimeter held 800 MHz longer (1.16–1.52 times), but G4-S needs 6 blocks and 4 give a 99% interval for the log
  ratio of [−0.11, 0.61]. The card idled at 71–76 °C for most of the window, so the heating sessions, which start only
  from 60 °C or less, ran only in the evening's cool spell; a cooler rest would be needed.
- **What hung aifoundry2's Master Minion** (28 September, E51). A launch during the governor's idle reset is a
  hypothesis only. The validation's three heating sessions (28 September 22:13–23:56 PDT, 52 launches, 7 of them
  0.49–0.58 s after the previous one ended) did not hang it.
- **Why two host-to-card DMA commands of one stream halve the rate** (E55). T35-S puts the loss in one stream (one
  command in each of two streams loses nothing); whether the runtime or the master minion's DMA worker serves the two
  in turn is not established.
- **What caps a shire at 128 B per cycle** (E57, R43). No registered theory survived on any of the three cards; C (a
  shared 128 B path, banks at 64, sub-banks at 32) missed one configuration of 20.
- **Whether PA[11] and PA[12] pick the controller** (E57, R33c INSUFFICIENT on all three cards), and whether the refresh
  domain is the controller on aifoundry1's card 1, where R33b failed (it passed on both validation cards).
- **Why two lines that differ only in PA[17] conflict on aifoundry2** (E57, R33a FAIL there). PA[17] is a column bit in
  the L50 map, and on aifoundry3 and card 1 such lines read as one row; on 19 September the anatomy's one-bit flip of
  PA[17] on the same card read as the same row too (−12 cycles: `2026-09-19-memprobe-aifoundry2/summary.json`
  `bits["17"]`), by a different method (one load, then its flipped partner). A readback of that card's controller
  address-map registers by the service processor (the hub's rung 26) would say whether its map differs.
- **The stride-256 energy excess on aifoundry2** (E57, E102). Its intervals include 0 (+35.7 and +31.1 pJ per 64 B), and
  T102's ratios (0.48, 0.42) fall below the registered band, where aifoundry3 and card 1 resolved +42 to +54.
- **Where the master and memory shires sit on aifoundry3 and aifoundry2 by timing** (E56). T-DIRECT failed its frozen
  rule on both (rms 4.7 and 4.47 cycles against 4), so the placements rest on card 1's development data; why the two
  validation cards' medians scatter more than card 1's is not established.
- **Whether the mesh's cost per bit·hop follows its supply voltage** (E54). No voltage has been written; the first
  write waits for the owner.
- **Why aifoundry1's card 1's SRAM rail averages over 0.54 s**, half as long as its other rails (E58; the PMIC
  firmware is T2's reading, not tested), and why its minion rail rose with 1.32 s and fell with 1.02 s in development.
- **Whether the perimeter's longer time to the trip holds on another card in sustained heating** (E52). It held on
  aifoundry1's card 1 in short bursts (PLACE-tS PASS); the sustained test (PLACE-t) is INSUFFICIENT: 7 of its 10 runs
  (10 of all 15 Tier L runs) did not reach 66 °C within the 150 s cap, and 3 of its 5 blocks had both runs cut off. A test with longer chains, or a start edge chosen so card 1's
  512-minion runs cross, would be a new pre-registration, not a re-analysis.
- **Why the perimeter lasts longer** (E52). The die's edges may shed heat faster, or the 34 sensors of the mean may see
  less of heat made beside the edges and the unsensed I/O and PCIe cells; the host reads only the whole-degree mean and
  two unnamed extremes, which cannot tell these apart.
- **Whether aifoundry1's card 1 has active power management off or a latched governor.** Both explain a clock that
  never rises; the power-up residency since boot would narrow it.
- **The version-3 items that stayed INSUFFICIENT (E35–E47).** Each lacks the kept passes or cycles it needs on a card:
  - **The relay sweep's shape on aifoundry3 (E36, LAT-R).** aifoundry3 kept 2 of 3 relay passes (four of its relay
    host processes exited with code 139), so the ranking by the longest hand-off and the per-card ratios are untested
    there; on aifoundry2 and aifoundry1's card 1 the item fails anyway, on the mirrored offsets (d and 32 − d 2.7% and
    2.6% apart against 2%) (`V3R/lat.json`, `.items[item=LAT-R]`).
  - **The per-shire idle voltage pattern as a per-monitor offset (E41, TEL-Q).** aifoundry2 and aifoundry1's card 1
    have two complete idle captures each, not three; aifoundry3 fails three of the item's parts (its pass-to-pass sd's
    99% upper bound is 1.72 mV against ≤ 0.5). The offset sentence is dropped. The pattern's pass-to-pass correlation
    is exactly 1.0 wherever it was computed, so the captures may repeat one reading (`V3R/tel.json`,
    `.items[item=TEL-Q]`).
  - **Whether the s ↔ s+16 ring draws less than spinning (E43, RL-X3).** Its sampler starved on all three cards
    (median 63–141 ms against the 60 ms rule), so its burst was dropped in all 18 passes (`V3R/rl.json`,
    `dropped_bursts`).
  - **aifoundry2's idle split at 73 °C (E44, IDLE-d).** Only two of the three short cycles cooled to 73 °C: minion
    11.07 [10.69–11.46], SRAM 1.99 [1.95–2.03], mesh 3.62 [3.42–3.83], unsensed 15.05 [14.56–15.54] W over those two
    (`V3R/idle.json`, `.items[item=IDLE-d]`).
  - **The idle law's leakage split and slow shape (E44, IDLE-L).** The overnight long-cooling passes never ran on any
    card, so aifoundry2's T_L, A80 and slope at 80 °C and aifoundry3's slope at 56 °C and offset are untested
    (`V3R/idle.json`, `.items[item=IDLE-L]`, `long_passes_used` empty).
  - Decided since the first reduction: RL-X4 (no verdict, FAIL), RL-f on all three cards (FAIL: at the low edge) and
    ABLB-2b's kernel clause ("the difference is the kernel" kept) were first reduced without inputs from E37, E38 and
    E46; the second reduction supplied them (AMENDMENTS.md, "Implementation note (26 Sep 2026, after the first
    reduction)"), and their values are in the Version 3 rows above.
